## AI systems

- **PPOlab** - From-scratch NumPy PPO with a corrected internal-timeout collector, separate bootstrap/trace masks, full GAE credit matrices and sign-dependent clip-gradient audits. Independent scalar JavaScript evaluates all 4,675 checkpoint parameters and real CartPole physics. Sixty-four Python tests, including all 50 earlier tests; seven Node/actual-Chrome tests, including all four earlier tests, which compare 26 full reports/12,408 numbers and all 14,871 original one-step observations. A new exact-rational extension shows the clipped surrogate is not a policy box: on the supplied control the best clipped score is 9/25 while an actual hard ratio box gives 1/5, and every unconstrained optimizer has q0 in [7/25,1] with exact total variation from 9/50 to 9/10. New free-running paths can diverge: the reference run differs on 377 actions despite matching all 30 return lengths. Original checkpoint/results retained; no training-improvement, speed, trajectory-equivalence, KL-bound or universal-solve claim.
  - Case study: [ppolab.md](ppolab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/ppolab](https://github.com/midya8/ppolab)
  - Live: [Boundary and policy inspector](https://midya8.github.io/ppolab/)

Local-first tools for evaluating, tracing, reviewing and planning LLM and agent workflows, plus the training, adaptation and alignment primitives underneath them — gradient boosting, Gaussian-process regression and expected improvement, entropic optimal transport, exact affine prefix scans and coupled dense-state derivative audits, real-digit training with exact robustness audits and baseline-relative attribution, exact joint-play obedience and private-signal audits, exact zero-sum game audits with shared-table interval certificates, sequential betting evidence with certified confidence-sequence inversion, exact finite-channel privacy audits, safe screening and exact regularization paths, influence-function deletion diagnostics, natural-gradient geometry, variational autoencoding, vector search, decoding strategies, quantization, low-rank adaptation, speculative decoding, embedding distribution tests, exact Shapley attribution, a transformer built from scratch, and inspectable tree learners with exact response partitions, budgeted whole-ensemble search and constrained counterfactual geometry, learned masked-digit reconstruction with strong observed-pixel controls, and real-data unsupervised clustering with full restart and matching/uniqueness audits, and nonnegative parts learning with exact fixed-basis objective certificates, and manual Gaussian-mixture density estimation with constrained-covariance EM, precision-space KKT audits and fixed-model input-gradient geometry, and manual soft-margin SVM learning with replayable pair updates, exact rational weak-duality bounds and editable 30-feature scores, and observed-only rating factorization with manual alternating ridge solves, exact rational references and finite conditional-response audits, and auditable classification forests with exact integer split ordering, complete bootstrap-count witnesses and out-of-bag scores that use only zero-draw trees, and normalized spectral clustering with manual affinity/Laplacian/k-means code, explicitly disclosed NumPy eigensolves, full degree-response derivatives and a basis-invariant projector tangent, and manual linear-chain conditional random fields with analytic likelihood gradients, exact normalization witnesses, hypothetical label-event conditioning, auditable joint posterior path sampling and nonlocal occurrence-count events.

- **TagLab** - Manual linear-chain conditional random fields with analytic likelihood gradients, log-space messages and all 327 optimization states replayed, built from scratch — it makes the normalizer part of the model instead of letting a library hide what the partition function does: a 39-parameter sequence score combines fixed observed features, FROM/TO label transitions and separate start/end scores; manual log-space forward/backward produces the global partition, node and adjacent-label marginals, and expected-minus-observed feature counts give the supervised conditional-likelihood gradient, with Viterbi and token-marginal decisions kept separate. The independent-label control uses exactly the same eight observed features including left/right context, its 15 sequence scores fixed at zero and its remaining 24 weights fitted, so it is a fitted competitor rather than a weak untrained baseline; both families fit three positive L2 penalties and select by validation conditional NLL per token. The losing metric stays visible: the selected chain's test NLL/token is 0.557614 against the independent control's 0.813715, but its Viterbi token errors are worse, 66/164 against 56/164, while marginal token errors tie at 56/164 and whole-sequence errors tie at 15/16 — a better distribution score and a worse best path are different objectives, and the family comparison changes fitted parameters and penalty geometry too, so it is descriptive rather than an isolated causal effect. The fixture is 24 training, 12 validation and 16 test sequences of noisy numeric observations from persistent synthetic states, with seeds, complete examples and labels retained. Every one of 327 stored states retains all parameters, all 39 analytic gradient entries, objective, gradient norm, accepted Armijo step and rejected larger steps; the browser independently recomputes objective and gradient from the original training examples. Three fits stop at the 1e-5 gradient tolerance and three exhaust their 80-update budget; the selected chain exhausts its budget with gradient norm about 0.000629, and convexity plus a decreasing curve do not certify that finite iterate as an optimum. The inspector is careful about what it changes and what it shows. It accepts custom symbol sequences and explicitly marked transition-score changes, recomputes messages, node and pair probabilities, complete best-path score, entropy and backpointers in scalar JavaScript, and never dresses the result up as something else: original labels show only while the symbols match the selected stored example, custom observations carry no invented labels, and modified scores are never presented as retraining or fresh test performance. Invalid input clears stale evidence instead of leaving old numbers on screen, and keyboard access, clipboard failure and success, and a real JSON download are all exercised, not assumed. The label-event inspector is careful the same way: the independent control fixes 15 sequence coordinates, so the derivatives it displays are ambient-chain coordinates, not trainable control weights; JSON uses null for exact forbidden log entries, with no fake finite sentinel and no NaNs; and conditioning can increase entropy, so a narrower event is not automatically more confidence. An exact normalization witness keeps the global-versus-local distinction concrete: three allowed paths A/C, B/A and B/C have raw weights [1/9,1,1/9], one global normalizer gives [1/11,9/11,1/11] while equal initial branch scores and local outgoing normalizers give [1/2,9/20,1/20], with five evidence weights computed in exact fractions. V0.2 adds hypothetical label-event conditioning: an allowed label subset at each position, fitted scores fixed, event mass plus conditional node/pair/path witnesses, all 39 log-event derivatives, an empty allowed set exactly impossible with no invented conditional distribution, and a possible rare event whose finite log probability underflows to zero in floating point, distinguished from zero support — 3,336 event witnesses and 4,647 browser states overall, with 31 Python and 12 Node/real-Chrome tests at v0.2. V0.3 adds auditable backward-conditional joint path sampling with a paired independent-node control using the same supplied uniforms; exact pair probabilities and total variation stay separate from descriptive frequencies of 64 seeded draws, each reverse step retaining its probabilities, log probabilities, uniform, CDF, chosen bin and numerical flags. V0.4 adds nonlocal occurrence-count events: the count inspector conditions on exactly r occurrences of a chosen label, and an augmented (count, label) forward/suffix recursion exposes the full original count distribution and reconstructs conditional node/pair probabilities, entropy, a complete best path and all 39 event derivatives — counts are positions, not runs or segments. An independent fair two-position control conditioned on exactly one A permits only AB and BA, each with mass 1/2; node marginals remain halves, but independent draws from them leave the event half the time, and the inspector separately computes the independent-node Poisson-binomial count law — conditioning can create dependence without transition scores, and a correct expected count does not guarantee a valid complete path. 43 Python and 20 Node/Chrome tests cover 600 events per enumerator across sixty fixtures in independent Fraction and BigInt path enumerators, all 39 finite-difference event derivatives, 864 cross-language numerical witnesses and 864 browser queries; the inspectors total 5,895 UI states/queries, and the original v0.1 suite's 22 Python and seven Node tests cover 60 rational path-potential controls, all 39 finite differences, 12,753 scalar gradient checks and 32 complete selected-model sequence witnesses, with real Chrome exercising 327 optimizer states plus 984 model/sequence/focus states. Eleven modules and nine commands reproduce outside the checkout from the installed wheel, including six fresh fits, 327 audits and four exact archive/loader exports, with a lossless three-part compressed archive whose SHA-256 is checked before parsing. This models p(labels|observations), not p(observations); both neighboring observations are available, so it is not streaming inference, and model posteriors are not calibrated real-world confidence — original synthetic sequences, not an NLP benchmark, and the portable xorshift32 mid-bin stream is deterministic, not cryptographic or certified independent entropy; the quadratic count-state computation has a 64-position limit, and near-ties may yield different Python/JavaScript path representatives. [Source](https://github.com/midya8/taglab). [Live sequence and gradient inspector](https://midya8.github.io/taglab/). [Label-event inspector](https://midya8.github.io/taglab/evidence.html). [Count inspector](https://midya8.github.io/taglab/counts.html). [Method and limits](https://github.com/midya8/taglab/blob/main/METHOD.md).
- **SpectralLab** - Normalized spectral clustering with manual affinity/Laplacian/k-means code and explicitly disclosed NumPy eigensolves, built from scratch — it makes the graph the visible choice instead of letting a scatter plot hide which points are neighbors and what their connection means: the affinity and normalized Laplacian are built explicitly, the eigenspace is retained, every row is normalized and manual k-means runs on the embedding, with NumPy supplying only the symmetric eigenvalue solve through LAPACK. The browser reconstructs all graph weights and every selected point's degree, neighbors, cut contributions and three eigenpair equations, with original coordinates and row-normalized embedding side by side; any actual archived graph or point can be selected, colors or a display-only edge threshold changed, and the full point witness exported as JSON — a threshold that hides an edge never changes the fit. Two original seeded 96-point fixtures use noisy interleaving arcs and concentric rings; nine fixed graph rules give 18 total fits — union/mutual kNN at 3/6/12 neighbors and full RBF at sigma .08/.25/1.0 — with raw-coordinate k-means alongside, two clusters and 12 starts fixed, least embedding inertia choosing only a k-means restart, and generator labels never choosing a graph or fit. The failures stay visible: moons union-3 reaches ARI 1.000 with eigengap .003156 while moons RBF sigma 1.0 gives ARI .242035 with the larger eigengap .350982 — a larger gap is not agreement with the intended classes, and raw-coordinate k-means gives the same .242035; rings union-6 reaches ARI 1.000 while rings union-12 gives ARI -.010195, so adding edges does not promise a better grouping; and moons mutual-3 has four disconnected components, so its two-dimensional truncation of a four-dimensional zero eigenspace is not unique and that fit is refused, with no arbitrary cluster labels shown. An original seven-vertex weighted graph has only 63 distinct nonempty binary cuts after fixing one cluster label; independent rational enumeration finds the optimum 3450/4181 = .8251614446304711, while the archived spectral relaxation plus k-means returns .860832137733142 — a worse cut, with both partitions and every weight visible, and no exact-optimality claim for the larger fixtures. V0.2 adds full degree-response derivatives and a basis-invariant projector tangent: a fixed undirected edge edit changes both endpoint degrees and every normalized incident edge, and manual differentiation retains those terms; the bottom-two spectral projector P = U Uᵀ has a basis-invariant derivative using only retained-to-excluded denominators, with internal repeated eigenvalues allowed and repeated eigenvalues crossing the retained boundary refused without an epsilon repair. Six original small graphs include four smooth base cases and 28 predeclared finite states, one reaching a five-cycle boundary tie; a six-cycle and an isolated vertex are refused at the base, while the two disconnected triangles retain their full zero eigenspace and have a valid one-sided graph-domain response when a missing bridge is added. Every actual finite eigensolve and 12-start k-means result is archived, not predicted from the derivative; shared-scale heatmaps compare actual ΔP with tP′; the archived tiny-step assignment change is explicitly a rounding-ambiguity control — distinct tested partitions have numerically equal inertia — not a unique-optimum, label-derivative, robustness-certificate or stability-rate claim. New independent checks include 83 normalized-Laplacian edge finite differences, 42 exact Fraction cut derivatives, analytic and repeated-block projector controls, basis rotations including an archived eigenspace rotation, one-sided Taylor error ratios and derivative-identity residuals. 33 Python tests include the sensitivity controls above and cover 528 independent Fraction-distance graph references, 558 rational cut references, analytic cycle spectra and energy identities, component/zero-degree/boundary-tie refusals, invariant subspace rotations, every fresh graph fit, source/degree/eigenvector/label/score mutations and strict CLI behavior; eigenspace comparisons use projectors, not signs. Nine Node/real-Chrome tests include the new sensitivity view and cover 18 reconstructed graphs, 4,896 scalar eigenpair-row equations, all fitted/raw cuts, separate BigInt enumeration of 63 tiny-graph cuts, exact browser dataset exports, 1,728 actual browser point/graph states, refusal clearing, physical keyboard changes that leave fit witnesses unchanged, clipboard success/failure, real JSON Blob downloads and 320/390/1280 layouts with 44-pixel controls. Scalar JavaScript checks 146 full-base eigenpair equations and 1,022 matrix row cells, and real Chrome visits 168 new case/step/row states. The installed wheel reproduces outside the checkout, importing all six modules, executing nine commands, refitting all 18 graphs, auditing fresh and archived reports and verifying four archived web exports byte-for-byte, including fresh and archived sensitivity audits; CI runs Python 3.10-3.13, package reproduction and real Chrome. These are dense full-data synthetic illustrations, not held-out prediction, exact discrete optimization, optimal-graph selection or calibrated uncertainty; eigenvector signs are arbitrary, zero-degree vertices are not silently connected, and numerical eigenpair residuals are checks, not formal proofs. Original synthetic code and data are MIT licensed; the method follows Ng, Jordan and Weiss (2001), with von Luxburg's tutorial and explicit NumPy primitive attribution in the method note. [Source](https://github.com/midya8/spectrallab). [Live graph inspector](https://midya8.github.io/spectrallab/). [Sensitivity inspector](https://midya8.github.io/spectrallab/sensitivity.html). [Equations and limits](https://github.com/midya8/spectrallab/blob/main/METHOD.md).
- **ForestLab** - Manual binary CART forests with exact integer split ordering and a live membership inspector, built from scratch — it keeps every bootstrap count visible instead of letting a vote fraction hide which trees saw the observation: binary CART split costs are compared by arbitrary-precision integer cross products, checked by sixty independent Fraction designs and a separate BigInt complete-mask enumerator, with explicit conventions for lower-observed thresholds, feature/cut ties, minimum leaves, pure nodes and nonpositive gains — exact node ordering is not a claim of a globally optimal tree. Eight 31-tree forests retain all 248 bootstrap vectors and 7,102 tree nodes; the browser recomputes all 31 paths and exposes every in-bag count for a stored training group, edited inputs drop group membership immediately, and an OOB score uses only trees with zero draws of that group — zero eligible trees means no prediction, never the full-forest result. The original licensed UCI file has 1,372 numeric records but only 1,348 unique four-feature vectors; exact copies are collapsed and kept together before the fixed 808/270/270 train/validation/test split, with original row IDs auditable, and no physical specimen identifier is available, so near-duplicates and shared specimens are not ruled out. Each model family selects by validation errors, then Brier score, then ID before test labels are read; all eight forests, four single trees and seven controls remain visible, and the selected forest makes 3/270 test errors against the stronger selected 5NN control at 0/270 and the selected single tree at 8/270 — one historical split, not a claim of zero future error or a test of real authentication, and the stronger control stays visible. 39 Python and 12 Node/real-Chrome tests check fresh fitting, source/group/split provenance, all bootstrap vectors, complete node witnesses, train-only standardization and logistic gradients; test-label poisoning leaves fits and selections unchanged, and source, split, bootstrap, gain, metric and selection mutations are rejected. An independent scalar JavaScript implementation checks 10,784 forest evaluations, 8,088 control scores and 6,464 OOB records; Chrome drives all 992 native path references and every model/partition state, physical-keyboard membership changes, invalid-input clearing/recovery, clipboard success/failure, actual JSON Blob download and 320/390/1280-pixel layouts; the installed ten-module package runs all ten commands, refits all 19 models, audits them and reproduces the checked browser exports outside the checkout. V0.2 adds [joint permutation diagnostics](https://midya8.github.io/forestlab/permutation.html): all 15 nonempty feature blocks move through the same 16 fixed row bijections, with selected columns from the same donor and recipient labels and remaining features in place; both frozen models, the selected forest and the stronger selected 5NN, retain all 240 cases and 129,600 model-row prediction counts, with every repeat including negative error changes visible. Baseline validation errors are 4/270 and 1/270, distinct from the unchanged test errors 3/270 and 0/270; no model is refitted, and repeat ranges are not confidence intervals. An exact three-row, six-bijection control exposes off-support singleton hybrids. The added diagnostic has independent scalar-JavaScript predictions for all 129,600 model-row evaluations, exact Fraction metric controls, full fresh regeneration, fit/map/count mutation rejection, train/test-label poisoning and 1,230 actual browser states; outside-checkout reproduction regenerates all 240 cases, audits them and compares seven archive/loader exports exactly. Classes stay the source's labels 0 and 1, not an invented genuine/forged mapping; these are historical precomputed image descriptors, not an image-upload classifier or a currency detector, and hard votes, leaf fractions and neighbor fractions are not calibrated probabilities, confidence intervals or conformal sets. OOB is a training-sample diagnostic, not the held-out full-forest test or independent specimen validation, and paths are not causal explanations. Original data: Lohweg (2012), UCI Banknote Authentication, DOI 10.24432/C55P57, CC BY 4.0; original bytes are retained in lossless checked text encoding, with exact-copy grouping and partitioning as this project's transformations, and no source images were copied; code is MIT, and established CART/forest methods are credited in the method note, where attribution is not endorsement. [Repository](https://github.com/midya8/forestlab). [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/forestlab/). [Method, sources and limits](https://github.com/midya8/forestlab/blob/main/METHOD.md). The v0.3 leaf-collision inspector exposes all-tree similarity as an explicit Gram matrix, with exact integer/BigInt sum-of-squares witnesses, and a separate jointly-OOB view keeps pair-specific tree counts with undefined comparisons left undefined: a constructed negative quadratic form (−2) and a triangle failure show why OOB normalization is not a universal kernel guarantee. All 512 small partition controls, 5,760 native/scalar-JS matrix entries, 1,152 actual pair states and 13,824 heatmap colors are checked; proximity is the mean of per-tree leaf collisions (P=ZZᵀ/T), squared feature-space distance is 2(1−P), and the three-row control gives cᵀP_oob c=−2 for c=(1,−2,1) against an all-tree form of 10/3. Frozen supervised model geometry, not semantic similarity, causal influence, uncertainty or a new score. [Geometry derivation and limits](https://github.com/midya8/forestlab/blob/main/PROXIMITY.md).
- **RatingLab** - Observed-only rating factorization with manual NumPy alternating ridge solves, built from scratch — it keeps the observed edges visible instead of filling unobserved pairs with zeros: rating 0 is an actual observation here, and fitting an unobserved pair as zero would invent evidence, so every joint bias-and-factor block is derived as a regularized linear system with the training mean fixed, the sum-squared objective applying the regularizer once per parameter rather than once per observation. Each user and item update checks its local objective decrease, with half-pass accounting telescoping to the global objective, and full gradients are kept separate from the residual of the last solved block; an all-zero-factor stationary control has objective 4 while an explicit feasible factorization has objective .4, so a zero gradient is not a global-minimum certificate. On 1,161 distinct user-item pairs from 138 users and 130 items, a frozen observed-edge split uses 696 training, 232 validation and 233 test rows, with all held-out entities warm; eight rank-2/rank-4 factor fits use two penalties and two seeds, and all eight exhaust their stationarity budget, while a separate bias-only family selects among three penalties with the same validation RMSE rule, and test rows are evaluated only after both selections, without refitting. The selected factor fit has test RMSE .656456774 against .656175974 for the selected bias-only control, with training mean .758069714 — the bias control stays visible even though it slightly wins, and this is one historical split, not a significance or general-superiority claim; the source rows have no timestamps, so the split is not chronological, and synthetic cold-entity tests do not establish measured cold-start performance. Sixty exact Fraction Gaussian-elimination references check the ridge solutions, finite differences check every coordinate of a tiny complete model gradient, all eleven saved candidates are fitted again, and poisoning every test label must leave fitting, histories and selection unchanged; an independent scalar JavaScript implementation checks 12,771 predictions against native references, and real Chrome exercises eleven models, 33 visible pairs, physical keyboard rotation, normal matrices, invalid-input recovery, clipboard success/failure, a JSON Blob download and 320/390/1280-pixel layouts. A shared orthogonal rotation preserves predictions and regularization while changing coordinate products, so those products are algebraic terms, not identified tastes, semantic attributes or causal importance, and the browser's 130-item strip keeps missing observations empty rather than pretending to be a validated recommendation ranking. The v0.2 conditional-response extension separates saved scores, a freshly re-solved unmodified ridge block and its finite rating edit or row deletion, exposing leverage, the positive deletion denominator, query derivatives and all-opposite-index response curves; sixty additional exact rational design systems, 2,784 direct deleted-block refits and 576 native/scalar-JavaScript witnesses check the formulas, with original factors and scores unchanged. The two conditional normal systems remain positive definite with λ > 0, including deletion of a block's sole observation; a target edit has exactly linear finite response within this quadratic problem, and Sherman-Morrison gives a deletion update scaled by the original conditional residual and 1 − h, with a bound 1 − h ≥ λ/(λ + ‖a‖²) following from the deleted system's ridge floor, while numerical real-data readouts are not rational or interval certificates. The saved user block may no longer be its conditional minimizer after the final item pass, and that movement is reported separately before any edit; the inspector's three displayed scores prevent a changed conditioning baseline from being mislabeled as an observation's effect, and a deleted-row residual is algebra, not a valid full-model cross-validation estimate, since the original opposite factors and training mean still encode the row. The mean and opposite factors remain frozen and retain information from the row: this is not full-model leave-one-out validation, causal influence, data unlearning or an uncertainty bound, and no original model, metric, split, source row or original figure is modified. 37 Python and 14 Node/actual-Chrome tests cover 60 independent exact rational ridge references, eleven fresh refits, test-label poisoning and 12,771 scalar/native predictions, with a ten-module installed wheel with eight main CLI commands and two single-row conditional-response module commands, reproducing all ten modules including the package root, eleven fits and both full audits outside the source checkout. Only the licensed rating CSV is bundled; consumer profiles, restaurant locations, names and demographics are not used, source identifiers become stable indices rather than features, and MovieLens was considered but not redistributed because of its license terms. Historical UCI restaurant and consumer data are CC BY 4.0; code is MIT, and missing pairs are not negatives, while observed-rating RMSE is not top-N relevance, missing-pair accuracy or calibrated certainty. [Repository](https://github.com/midya8/ratinglab). [Live inspector](https://midya8.github.io/ratinglab/). [Conditional edit/deletion inspector](https://midya8.github.io/ratinglab/influence.html). [Objective, equations, protocol and limits](https://github.com/midya8/ratinglab/blob/main/METHOD.md). [Derivation and limits](https://github.com/midya8/ratinglab/blob/main/INFLUENCE.md). [Original UCI dataset](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/dataset/232/restaurant+consumer+data). The v0.3 joint-batch inspector changes or removes multiple original training rows within one frozen block. Target edits add; joint deletions solve a coupled Schur system and generally differ from a sum of separate-original-baseline deletions. It shows the full source-bound problem, conservative positive-ridge bound, numerical condition/refusal checks and all-query joint/separate curves. Independent checks add 320 rational direct-system cases, 128 real augmented-least-squares cases and 320 complete native/browser Cholesky responses plus 4 controls. 37 Python and 14 Node/actual-Chrome tests, ten-module wheel and eight main CLI plus two single-row module commands. Original fits, scores and stronger bias control stay unchanged. This remains a conditional calculation, not full ALS retraining, cross-validation, causal attribution, uncertainty or data unlearning. [Joint batch inspector](https://midya8.github.io/ratinglab/batch.html). [Derivation and limits](https://github.com/midya8/ratinglab/blob/main/BATCH.md).
- **MarginLab** - Manual linear/RBF soft-margin SVM learning with replayable pair updates and editable 30-feature scores, built from scratch — it keeps the constraints visible instead of letting a solver's claim stand alone: the original two-variable dual solver handles linear and RBF kernels with exact bounded line maxima and zero-curvature endpoints, and every accepted pair retains its indices, changes, curvature and objective gain, with a separate replay checking all constraints, endpoints and the objective telescope. Training-only scaling, a frozen 341/113/115 split and validation-only selection prevent test-based selection; the full-30D nearest-neighbor control is not weakened to the displayed two dimensions, and all eight fits remain visible, including two with a better test score than the validation choice. A tiny independent Fraction active-face KKT oracle checks 60 optima without calling the pair solver, and full-data stopping remains numerical, not an exact-rational certificate. Four separable one-dimensional points reach 100% training accuracy after one pair update while the optimization gap is still 2/3; opposite labels at an identical input give zero pair curvature and require endpoint comparison; duplicate same-label inputs have nonunique support coefficients at the same decision function — correct labels, unique causal importance and a solved optimization are different claims. The certificate extension repairs the saved binary64 dual equality before proving exact rational weak-duality bounds for all four represented linear training problems: the original saved floating-point coefficients have nonzero exact signed dual mass, so their represented binary64 inputs are converted to Fractions, clipped to the box, and coefficients in the excess class reduced until the equality is exactly zero, recording the original residual and total movement without changing the original model. A separate exact feasible primal and repaired feasible dual enclose each represented linear training optimum, with approximate gap readouts for C=.01,.1,1,10 of 7.97e-8, 1.42e-6, 2.90e-5 and 3.23e-4 — the full canonical objective ratios, not those decimals, are the bounds. Two independently implemented readers, Python Fraction and browser BigInt, check all constraints, objectives and repair accounting without importing the optimizer; sixty additional active-face exact optima must lie inside the produced intervals, and the browser also checks all four full proofs against every original standardized input, label, C, coefficient and bias. A changed inactive row is an explicit control: its proof can remain mathematically valid for its changed declared problem while source matching rejects it, so proof validity does not authenticate data or authorship. 36 Python and 15 Node/real-Chrome tests cover 120 exhaustive 1-5D corner problems, all 256 extremal scores recomputed from the exact original dual sum, 128 complete native/browser responses and 128 actual Chrome UI combinations, plus the 60 exact active-face optima, four full proofs and eight fresh fits, with four Python CI versions and an 11-module/nine-command installed wheel plus the box exporter reproducing all eight fresh fits and full saved/fresh pair audits; the browser checks all 4,552 native/scalar-JavaScript scores, every feature, 24 UI model/example states, physical keyboard input, blocked/successful clipboard, actual JSON Blob download and 320/390/1280 layouts including horizontal-table reachability. The plot is a two-feature slice with the other 28 features at their training means, not a projection, and algebraic score terms are not causal effects. Original UCI Wisconsin Diagnostic data is CC BY 4.0 with exact source hashes; source metadata is losslessly encoded to preserve its original missing final newline. This is a bound for the represented standardized linear problem, not exact real-number standardization, RBF kernels, generalization error, clinical reliability or a claim that an exact optimum was attained; the original numerical inspector stays numerical, with no prediction, score, split, pair history or original figure changed. Educational historical data, not clinical validation, calibrated certainty, generalization bounds or source authentication. [Live 30-feature inspector](https://midya8.github.io/marginlab/). [Live exact linear proof inspector](https://midya8.github.io/marginlab/certificate.html). [Equations, source attribution and limits](https://github.com/midya8/marginlab/blob/main/METHOD.md). [Derivation, repair algorithm and limits](https://github.com/midya8/marginlab/blob/main/CERTIFICATE.md). The input-box extension adds exact finite original-unit input boxes: the frozen represented linear models define an exact affine function in original feature units, so coordinate signs yield its exact minimum and maximum on a finite rational box with complete corner vectors attaining each, both label claims use the existing +1-at-zero rule, and independent native Fraction and browser BigInt readers check all coordinate support equalities and scores, with the page separately reconstructing the affine function from every original saved coefficient, represented training input, mean, scale and bias, and valid changed-function controls keeping source identity separate from mathematics. Presets expand eight fixed historical queries by exact plain-decimal multiples of training-only scales — synthetic independent-coordinate sets, not measurement tolerances, domain-valid inputs or plausible causal interventions — and all 30 intervals can be edited, with the declared center required to remain inside and edits, malformed inputs and source mismatches clearing old results and blocking export; approximate plots include zero and stay separate from the full exact witness fractions and unchanged scalar floating center scores. New checks add 120 exhaustive 1-5D corner problems, all 256 extremal scores independently recomputed from the exact original dual sum, 128 complete native/browser responses and 128 actual Chrome UI combinations, covering zero coefficients/nonunique corners, asymmetric intervals, point boxes, ties, strict schemas, stale/invalid states, physical keyboard, blocked/successful copy, actual JSON Blob and mobile layout; four bounded artifact chunks and a small source-affine declaration reproduce without fitting, while the existing 60 exact active-face optima, full pair replay, training-objective proofs and holdout-poisoning checks remain. Original fits, holdout scores, split and exact training-objective proofs are unchanged; the exact rational affine function is not a bound on rounded production inference or exact real-number standardization, and no RBF, clinical, probability, causal, generalization, domain-validity or authenticity claim is made. [Live exact input-box inspector](https://midya8.github.io/marginlab/box.html). [Support-function derivation, reproduction and limits](https://github.com/midya8/marginlab/blob/main/BOX.md).
- **MixtureLab** - Manual Gaussian-mixture EM on real, licensed chemistry, built from scratch — it keeps the likelihood honest instead of letting a bigger model claim a win: on UCI Wine (Aeberhard & Forina 1992), 178 chemical analyses with 13 features, the loader strips the cultivar target before returning features, so no labels enter splitting, fitting or scoring; a fixed label-free split uses 106 training, 36 validation and 36 test rows, scaling is training-only, both covariance families, k in {1,2,3,5} and three fixed seeds yield 24 fits, with a covariance floor of 0.05 in squared standardized units. Training likelihood chooses each restart, validation NLL chooses the configuration, and test scores open afterward without a validation refit, with all candidates staying visible: the validation pick, Diagonal k=5 seed 11, scores 13.40992 / 13.45753 / 15.34782 (train/validation/test NLL); the fitted-Gaussian control, Full k=1 seed 11, scores 14.40496 / 14.99046 / 15.72686; and the best-training configuration, Full k=5 seed 23, scores 10.20168 / 17.95955 / 18.47913 — more capacity improves training density here while worsening test density, and this one small historical split does not show general superiority or statistical significance. With a fixed covariance constraint C >= 0.05I, the full-covariance M-step clips weighted scatter eigenvalues; adding 0.05I is a different operation and generally does not solve that subproblem. The method derives the precision-space KKT conditions and checks them numerically on 100 random scatters, with 40 independent analytic 2D clipping references. Every EM update keeps the old posterior and records mean likelihood gain = complete-data Q gain + posterior KL; all initial/final models, complete scalar histories and final before/after transition witnesses are retained. All 24 fits meet the stated likelihood-increment tolerance — that is not a stationary-point test or a global optimum certificate — and zero numerical component mass fails explicitly, with no hidden reseeding. A separate scalar JavaScript Cholesky implementation recomputes density and every component responsibility for arbitrary edited 13-feature vectors; training-only PCA supplies a view, not the fit, and its ellipses are 90% probability contours of the model's 2D Gaussian marginals, not full-dimensional decision regions or measured coverage. The [live 13-feature density inspector](https://midya8.github.io/mixturelab/) exposes all eight model choices, all 24 restart summaries, the complete current-fit trajectory, JSON witness copy/download and clear invalid-input errors. The far-input control shows how a nearly certain within-model posterior can accompany very low density. 36 Python tests and eight Node/real-Chrome tests cover independent scalar/2D Gaussian likelihoods, weighted moments, precision-space KKT checks, analytic covariance clipping, EM identities, holdout-poisoning isolation, original-unit density transformations, all eight models x 178 source rows (1,424 native/browser evaluations), every responsibility, edited inputs, physical keyboard behavior, copy/download and 320/390/1280 layouts; a built wheel imports ten modules and runs all seven CLI commands outside the checkout, including all 24 fresh fits and saved/fresh audits. The fixed-model geometry extension adds a [live gradient, curvature and ascent inspector](https://midya8.github.io/mixturelab/geometry.html) computing all 13 input-score coordinates, the complete 13x13 Hessian, the kx13 component-posterior Jacobian and original-unit transforms — fixed-model input derivatives, not parameter training or causal feature importance — with a chosen finite coordinate change compared against first- and second-order predictions. A normalized-gradient line search accepts only an actual log-density gain at least 0.0001 times step length times gradient norm, retains every accepted point and stops explicitly on norm, budget or line-search exhaustion; eight traces use the same already-public input at budget 128, where the four diagonal models and full k=1 reach the norm criterion while full k=2,3,5 remain budget-exhausted, and no mode is certified. Two equally weighted unit Gaussians at -2 and +2 have zero input gradient and curvature +3 at zero; the routine stops there at a strict minimum, visibly demonstrating why its norm rule is not a mode certificate. Independent checks include 312 all-coordinate finite-difference probes, every posterior-Jacobian and Hessian column, 50 scalar directional second differences, affine/rotation/permutation checks, 40 complete native/browser geometry cases and eight trace references; new tests recreate only the two new reference artifacts, checking every nested numeric value with explicit tolerance and preserving keys, shapes, statuses and counts exactly. All original models, experiment report, source data, split, scales, browser references, browser dataset and score figure remain byte-for-byte unchanged. No cultivar prediction, wine-quality score, calibrated uncertainty, identified latent cause, global mixture optimum, floor/split sensitivity guarantee or population-wide conclusion. Data are CC BY 4.0; code is MIT. [Derivation, frozen protocol and source ledger](https://github.com/midya8/mixturelab/blob/main/METHOD.md). [Derivative and search derivation](https://github.com/midya8/mixturelab/blob/main/GEOMETRY.md). The frozen conditional inference extension adds a [live observed-coordinate conditional inspector](https://midya8.github.io/mixturelab/conditional.html) that conditions any observed-coordinate subset under the same frozen model: each component uses observed-marginal evidence, a Schur-complement conditional mean/covariance and log-space posterior weights, with complete within/between covariance, raw-unit Jacobians and zero-dimensional limits in the numerical witness. Native Cholesky solves and scalar browser loops add no ridge or conditional-covariance clipping, and numerical breakdown is refused rather than repaired. The mean can lie in a low-density gap: a separate diagonal-mixture control increases target variance from 1.09 to 25.1 by changing posterior component weights while each component target variance stays 0.1. 45 Python and eleven Node/Chrome tests include 100 separate precision-block Gaussian oracles and direct scalar Gaussian formulas, all 5,696 original model/row/four-mask density factorizations and moment decompositions, 160 complete native/scalar-JS references and all 160 through the UI. Twelve-module/nine-command installed reproduction retains all 24 original fresh refits and audits. Analytical masks on complete source data, not real missingness validation, causal intervention, calibrated intervals or new accuracy. [Conditional algebra and sources](https://github.com/midya8/mixturelab/blob/main/CONDITIONAL.md).
- **PartsLab** - Nonnegative parts learning with manual NumPy factorization, built from scratch — it keeps the strong controls in view instead of letting the factorization grade itself: six fits on all 3,823 original UCI training digits without labels, ranks 8/16/32 with seeds 11/23 and 100 outer / 3 inner coordinate sweeps, lowest reencoded training MSE selecting the restart within each rank; training-only restart selection and a frozen model/protocol hash verified before evaluation opens the 1,797-row official holdout. Rank-matched signed PCA and SVD beat NMF on test MSE at every fixed rank — rank 8: 0.0309683 vs 0.0251267/0.0257206; rank 16: 0.0163996 vs 0.0118776/0.0119639; rank 32: 0.0046791 vs 0.0027689/0.0027681 — against a training-mean baseline of 0.0735163, so no NMF-superiority claim is made. All six joint fits exhaust their fixed stationarity budget rather than meeting joint stationarity tolerance, so decreasing loss is not convergence; frozen-basis NNLS is convex and all official test rows meet the numerical per-row KKT threshold in every saved run, while an arbitrary input may exhaust 1,000 sweeps and is marked unfinished. A plain multiplicative-update control with target [1,1], identity basis and initial coefficients [1,0] stays at loss 0.5 with a blocked gradient -1 where coordinate minimization reaches zero, isolating zero-locking; positive rescaling, shared permutations and duplicate basis rows show additive parts are not unique semantic explanations. 38 Python and eleven Node/real-Chrome tests include independent active-set oracles, finite-difference gradients, 35 support-enumerated NNLS optima, full test-loader-prohibited refits and exact SVG regeneration, with native and browser decompositions agreeing on all 120 model/example pairs and real Chrome checking all 2,048 pixels in the 32-part contribution grid; a ten-module/ten-CLI installed wheel reproduces all six fits and held-out scores. A [live parts inspector](https://midya8.github.io/partslab/) solves actual new nonnegative coefficients for editable pixels in scalar JavaScript, showing individual contributions, a separately rescaled basis view, numerical KKT status and unclipped reconstruction errors. The fixed-basis certificate adds exact binary64 primal/dual arithmetic, outward-rounded objective intervals and portable JSON proofs with an independent Python Fraction verifier: eighty exact rational active-set optima and all 120 cross-language witness references are checked, the browser verifies its own candidates including zero-sweep unfinished solves and accepts valid pasted proofs while rejecting tampered ones; the bounds are half squared error rather than per-pixel MSE, a residual-shift bound can be loose, and even zero gap does not imply unique coefficients. Data are Alpaydin & Kaynak (1998), UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits, DOI 10.24432/C50P49, CC BY 4.0; code is MIT. [Equations, protocol and limits](https://github.com/midya8/partslab/blob/main/METHOD.md). [Certificate proof and limits](https://github.com/midya8/partslab/blob/main/CERTIFICATE.md). The exact fixed-face response adds an [exact fixed-face pixel inspector](https://midya8.github.io/partslab/face.html): nominate a face from the numeric NNLS result, re-solve it exactly, and intersect every affine KKT inequality with the pixel domain; all endpoint ties, singular refusals, singleton faces and duplicate-basis nonuniqueness stay explicit. 38 Python and eleven Node/Chrome tests cover 200 independently enumerated tiny face candidates, all 120 stored nominations, 54 complete native/BigInt/Worker witnesses and 10,368 endpoint pixel colors; ten modules/ten commands with all six fresh fits reproduced. Original data, models and scores remain unchanged. One candidate interval, not a full path, unique support, robustness or joint-NMF optimum. [Math and limits](https://github.com/midya8/partslab/blob/main/FACE.md).
- **ClusterLab** - Real-data unsupervised clustering with manual k-means++/Lloyd updates, built from scratch — it makes the two classic k-means mistakes hard to miss: forty complete restart histories stay visible, learned centroids show mixed true-digit membership, and the browser recomputes membership for editable digits instead of replaying saved predictions. Four fixed arms — raw geometry with k-means++ starts, raw geometry with uniform starts, fitting-only PCA16 and whitened PCA16 — each choose minimum fitting inertia among seeds 100–109, never a test-label score; all 3,823 original UCI training digits fit without labels and the 1,797-row official holdout is audited with permutation-invariant scores and all-row silhouettes, giving holdout ARI .662690/.665149/.669707/.665596 across the four arms. Careful seeding has slightly lower raw fitting inertia (9682.7571 vs uniform 9683.1241) but uniform has slightly higher test ARI, so no general seeding win is claimed; PCA and whitening change the distance metric, so cross-geometry inertia/silhouette differences are descriptive and cannot be ranked as one unchanged objective. A [live clustering inspector](https://midya8.github.io/clusterlab/) projects edited 8x8 inputs and computes distances to all ten learned centers locally, with keyboard controls, a centroid atlas, held-out composition, all restart curves and JSON copy/download; the first twenty original test rows are included by index, not success. 48 Python tests plus thirteen Node/real-Chrome tests cover independent scalar objective and D² sampling references, pair/null-permutation ARI, joint-entropy NMI, all-pair silhouette, PCA eigenspectrum and whitening checks, a full 40-fit reproduction with test-loader prohibition, frozen model/protocol hashes verified before evaluation opens the test file, and all 80 public model/example pairs, with a ten-module/six-CLI installed wheel and CI on Python 3.10–3.13. The deliberate failure case shows why descent is not a global certificate: for [0,1,4,5,9,10] with k=2, initialization [0,1] converges to inertia 26.5 while exhaustive exact-rational enumeration of all 31 nonempty binary partitions gives the global optimum 17.5. The v0.2 restart inspector exposes all forty saved models, 180 distinct within-arm restart comparisons and forty self-pairs. A manual integer Hungarian solver supplies optimal center permutations and primal/dual witnesses, independently checked against subset-DP optima for all 220 matrices; browser BigInt checks those exact saved rounded-cost certificates, with 800 native/scalar-JavaScript inference pairs and all 400 directed pair controls tested. In raw k-means++, seeds 106 and 107 disagree most on fitting partitions (ARI .556972), while seed 107 remains the original lowest-inertia choice; geometric matching can visibly disagree with pointwise assignments, and same-data initialization sensitivity is not sampling stability or useful-class discovery. Original fits and holdout scores remain unchanged. [Open the live restart inspector](https://midya8.github.io/clusterlab/stability.html). [Restart matching method](https://github.com/midya8/clusterlab/blob/main/STABILITY.md). The v0.3 ambiguity audit adds 2,200 exact selected-edge exclusion witnesses, best-distinct permutations and optimal-matching counts. Independent full-cost dynamic programs check all 220 gaps and counts; browser BigInt verifies all 4,000 oriented exclusions. The matching is unique in every saved real matrix, while tied and unused-tight-edge controls expose why an optimum alone proves neither uniqueness nor a large margin. A strict sufficient cost-entry perturbation bound is not a sharp radius, pixel robustness or statistical uncertainty; the live inspector now shows exact optimal-permutation counts, the cheapest distinct permutation, every selected-edge exclusion and a smallest-gap control, and all ten exclusion certificates together cover every possible replacement without adding their overlapping solution counts. All original fits, scores and earlier matching witnesses remain unchanged. [Uniqueness and gap proof](https://github.com/midya8/clusterlab/blob/main/AMBIGUITY.md). Data are Alpaydin & Kaynak (1998), UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits, DOI 10.24432/C50P49, CC BY 4.0; code is MIT. Cluster IDs are groups, not digit labels; distances are not probabilities — no classifier accuracy, correct-k claim, sampling-stability estimate or global-optimality certificate is made.
- **DenoiseLab** - Learned masked-digit reconstruction with manual NumPy backprop, built from scratch — it keeps the strong controls in view instead of letting the network grade itself: on all 1,797 official UCI test digits, with error measured only on missing pixels in units normalized from [0,16] to [0,1], nearest neighbor (0.0312412) and PCA ridge (0.0317004) both beat the neural net (0.0353774) at 16 scattered missing pixels, while the network is lower at 32 (0.0340515 against PCA 0.0410443 and neighbor 0.0363968); at 48 scattered pixels the three are nearly tied (neural 0.0546866, PCA 0.0547961, neighbor 0.0549125) and on a 4-by-4 block the network leads (0.0649021 against neighbor 0.0650337 and PCA 0.0723497). The pixel-mean baseline sits far behind in every case — 0.0727401, 0.0735652 and 0.0733333 on the three scattered patterns, 0.1011151 on the block — and no universal ranking is inferred. The network takes 64 erased values plus 64 missing flags through tanh widths 64, 16, 64 and 64 sigmoid outputs, 14,544 learned parameters, with every derivative written out including hidden-only MSE and weight L2 and explicit Adam; training hides exactly 32 randomly selected pixels per image, refreshed on every presentation, and epoch candidates 10, 30 and 60 are selected on validation alone, with 60 winning as validation hidden-pixel MSE falls from 0.1797185 at initialization to 0.0320862. The official 3,823 training / 1,797 test split stays intact, with a deterministic class-stratified feature-hash split giving 3,062 fitting / 761 validation rows and no exact feature duplicates across partitions. The PCA control fits its mean/SVD basis on fitting rows only, selects rank 16 and ridge 0.1 from rank 8/16/32 and ridge .001/.01/.1/1 on the same validation masks, and infers coefficients from observed coordinates only with ridge and output clipping — not a deliberately weak zero-filled projection — while nearest neighbor scores squared error on visible pixels only with lowest fitting index on ties, and labels are used for the split, never fitting or prediction. A [live reconstruction inspector](https://midya8.github.io/denoiselab/) recomputes the neural denoiser, the observed-pixel PCA ridge solve, the exact fitting-example neighbor and the pixel mean in the browser when arbitrary pixels are erased, with independent scalar JavaScript inference. 27 Python tests plus four Node/real-Chrome tests cover 200 full-parameter finite differences against a separate objective, complete refit with a test-loader prohibition, full evaluation reproduction, all 40 public test/mask examples and responsive keyboard/copy/download controls, with a seven-module/four-CLI installed wheel and CI refitting and evaluating the installed package outside the checkout. It runs on Python 3.10+ with NumPy 2.2.6 and no other runtime dependency. Data are Alpaydin & Kaynak (1998), UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits, DOI 10.24432/C50P49, CC BY 4.0; code is MIT. One historical dataset, small images, one model/seed/budget and fixed artificial missingness, one mask per image/case, and no repeated-sample confidence interval — masked reconstruction error does not establish semantic preservation, calibrated uncertainty, image generation, clinical restoration or behavior on naturally missing observations, and this is a standalone reconstruction experiment, not DigitLab's classifier/attack study or LatentLab's synthetic variational density model. The v0.2 fixed-mask response extension derives input response from the fixed parameters — full chain-rule matrices through the three tanh layers and sigmoid for the network, and an observed-only linear-system derivative for PCA — with actual edited reconstructions kept separate from local tangent predictions, compared on shared-scale signed heatmaps, and hidden targets staying erased. Saturated PCA rows have zero local response, and nonconstant clipping-boundary rows are null rather than an invented derivative; nearest-neighbor response retains the old and new retrieved indices and a frozen-original-neighbor image, with a scalar switch control. Complete unbounded tangents and residuals can be copied or downloaded; only the rendered image clamps them. The suite grows to 35 Python and eight Node tests, covering derivative checks including independent finite differences and augmented least squares, 120 full cross-language responses, 480 browser views and 8,192 heatmap pixel checks, with an eight-module/five-command installed reproduction, and the original models, holdout scores and stronger winning controls stay unchanged. [Equations, independent checks and source ledger](https://github.com/midya8/denoiselab/blob/main/RESPONSE.md) · [Live input-response inspector](https://midya8.github.io/denoiselab/response.html). V0.3 adds exact scalar retrieval: for a single observed-pixel edit, the complete retrieval path over every open interval plus the complete endpoint tie, with Fraction/BigInt witnesses, a separate intended-source verifier, all-row affine dominance, and coincident rows and point-only winners visible. The suite grows to 44 Python and eleven Node tests, covering 5,344 independent rational oracle queries, 40 complete cross-language paths, 160 browser queries and 30,720 actual canvas colors, with a nine-module/seven-command installed reproduction. The guarantee is for one edited pixel with the other inputs and the mask fixed — not a multicoordinate radius or binary64 tie guarantee. [Scalar paths](https://midya8.github.io/denoiselab/path.html). V0.4 extends the retrieval map to two observed values: the [two-pixel retrieval map](https://midya8.github.io/denoiselab/plane.html) shows exact rational closed minimum cells over two observed values — area, segment-only and point-only cells, with explicitly proved empty regions — with Fraction/BigInt witnesses retaining all 3,062 original fitting-row coefficients. A source-matched verifier checks supporting halfplanes, maximal lower-dimensional cells and nonnegative infeasibility combinations without rerunning clipping, and shared boundaries use complete all-row exact tie queries rather than an interior representative. The suite grows to 52 Python and fourteen Node/real-Chrome tests, including independent boundary-pair enumeration of 238 small-source cells and 2,110 rational queries, forty complete cross-language maps, 160 browser queries and 30,720 actual canvas colors, with responsive 320/390/1280 layouts and a ten-module/nine-command installed reproduction. The map is a fixed two-coordinate slice only — not general robustness, causal recourse, uncertainty, better accuracy or binary64 tie equivalence. [Geometry, proof and limits](https://github.com/midya8/denoiselab/blob/main/PLANE.md).
- **TreeLab** - Inspectable tree learners and constrained counterfactual geometry, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes a frozen model's response to one feature, and the closest input reaching a target output band, inspectable as exact geometry, on one historical wine-quality table where every interval, witness and cost is an exact binary64 or rational value, not a claim about causal effects, plausible wines or new accuracy. The v0.6 extension searches the whole 32-tree ensemble at once instead of one tree: a best-first queue intersects all 32 unchanged trees, propagates forced branches through every member, and bounds the exact weighted-L1 objective from below by projecting onto the common feature box, with descendant output ranges pruning impossible target bands — so a finished search proves optimality or infeasibility under the supplied constraints, and a search that exhausts its budget is reported unfinished, never rounded up to an answer. The fixed 36-case audit crosses rows 1, 8 and 11, target bands [6,7] and [7,8], all-feature versus alcohol-only policies and 0/128/4,096 expansion budgets: at row 1, target [6,7], the alcohol-only search finishes with output 6.05953621031746 and cost 0.36290323, while the global all-feature search at 4,096 expansions remains unfinished with objective bounds [0.01838427, 0.36290323]; alcohol-only target [7,8] is proved infeasible, while the all-feature search retains a coarse expensive corner witness and a large gap, and no failure outcome is dropped. Optional seeds are checked against the bounds, immutable features and native full-ensemble inference, and the real demo uses already-known one-axis target witnesses as checked warm starts. The [live ensemble inspector](https://midya8.github.io/treelab/forest.html) displays every proof state, exact rational bounds, the full input and all 32 member outputs and leaf IDs, budget comparisons and source hashes. The 101 Python tests and five Node/real-Chrome tests cover independent tiny-forest exhaustive grids, budget-gap checks, every budget bound, native prediction re-scoring, subnormal midpoint and cost controls, strict requests, member inference and responsive inspector controls, with nine-module/nineteen-CLI installed-wheel checks; the method, proof scope and sources are in the source FOREST_SEARCH.md. Exact objective bounds apply to stored binary64 costs and single-input NumPy ensemble predictions — not exact training arithmetic, causal actionability, plausible chemistry, calibrated uncertainty or a scalable forest solver. The v0.5 extension adds constrained multi-feature model counterfactuals for the unchanged selected CART: find the closest input reaching a closed output band, subject to closed feature bounds, immutable indices and positive weighted L1 costs, by enumerating every target-output leaf, intersecting its path with the constraints and clamping the source into each feasible binary64 box. Strict right branches use nextafter, never an arbitrary epsilon; exact rational costs prevent false rounded ties and underflow; exact ties break by leaf ID; every candidate is re-routed. The fixed demo retains 36 requests — original test rows 1, 8 and 11, target bands [6,7], [7,8] and [9,9], all features, alcohol only, fixed alcohol and all fixed — with fitting-only min/max and reciprocal-range weights and no new fitting or scoring: for row 1, reaching [7,8] changes fixed acidity, volatile acidity and alcohol, with output 7.647058823529412 and cost about 0.27508758, alcohol alone cannot reach that band under the same bounds, and [9,9] has no target-output leaf. A [live constrained counterfactual explorer](https://midya8.github.io/treelab/counterfactual.html) displays every frozen request, feature delta, feasible candidate, full input/box/path and exact cost, with copy and local download including the complete request and report/model SHA-256 provenance. The 87 Python tests and four Node/real-Chrome tests include an independent exhaustive Cartesian-grid optimum check, exact and false rounded ties, subnormal costs, immutable and directional bounds, all real witnesses and responsive mobile controls, with eight-module/sixteen-CLI installed-wheel checks retaining all original data, models, historical controls and three audits; the method, CLI, proof scope and attribution are in the source COUNTERFACTUALS.md. This is a global weighted-L1 optimum over finite binary64 inputs for one stored CART under independent box constraints — not causal recourse, plausible chemistry, a feasible path, a bagging-ensemble optimum, calibrated uncertainty or a guarantee for outcomes in reality. The v0.4 extension is a live, frozen response explorer: the page bundles the complete source report and its SHA-256 without recomputing or rounding its intervals, witnesses or member outputs, with no API calls, accounts, analytics, remote inference or uploads, starting with row 1, the 32-tree ensemble and alcohol. Choose row, model and feature and inspect the full responsive step curve, exact interval inclusion, every member output and leaf ID and attainable min/max with complete input witnesses; the slider supports keyboard arrows, Previous/Next and a full interval list offer alternatives, and copy and local JSON download retain source row, model, feature, domain and interpretation warning, with denied clipboard access producing an explicit fallback rather than a false success. The 72 Python tests and three Node/real-Chrome tests include every one of the 66 UI slice selections and 1,028 segment witnesses and member displays, extrema, keyboard movement, copy success and denial, download names and expanded disclosures at 320/390/1280px, with chart labels reflowing for phones instead of shrinking desktop text; the live site passes the same real-Chrome checks and a byte-for-byte comparison of all five code/data/style files, and CI keeps Python 3.10-3.13 and installed-wheel seven-module/thirteen-CLI reproduction. All prior data, models, reports and three audit figures are unchanged; this is an interactive view of stored model-response geometry, not new inference, a new accuracy result, causality, calibrated uncertainty, realistic-wine guarantees or joint-feature robustness. The v0.3 extension computes exact one-feature response partitions: the first three historical test rows, original CSV indices 1, 8 and 11, all eleven features and both frozen models give 66 slices and 1,028 leaf segments, with domains from fitting-only min/max and rows 1 and 8 retained as exact copies rather than filtered for a more varied-looking demonstration. At non-axis splits, follow only the branch compatible with the fixed row; at axis splits, intersect the leaf's interval; combine all members' boundaries, average outputs on each common segment and return attainable extrema with complete input witnesses. Left routing is <=, upper endpoints are inclusive, the first lower endpoint is inclusive and later ones are exclusive, and open lower witnesses use math.nextafter, never an arbitrary epsilon. For the first test row, the complete alcohol slice has 3 selected-CART leaf segments and 50 ensemble segments, with model outputs ranging 5.31579-6.36283 for CART and 5.09926-6.54844 for bagging — model responses on a one-axis domain, not uncertainty intervals, causal improvements, possible-wine guarantees or multifeature robustness bounds, since correlated inputs may make synthetic feature combinations implausible. An opposite-stump control shows why member extrema are insufficient: each member can separately reach 10, but their mean is always 5, so the implementation uses common-axis leaf intersections, not the sum of separate extrema. The 70 tests include direct checks of all real-data segment boundaries, member leaf IDs and predictions, independent multidimensional leaf-box intersections, random small trees and ensembles, zero-width domains, repeated axes, nextafter boundaries, strict JSON/CLI and a no-refit complete report rerun, with the installed wheel exercising seven modules and all thirteen CLI modes and the audit SVG retaining its complete displayed curves, table data and regeneration checks. The v0.2 extension adds group-bootstrap bagging: draw 2,376 fitting-feature groups 2,376 times with replacement for each tree, then expand every drawn group with multiplicity, with grown members keeping minimum leaf size 5 and depth limit 20 and all feature columns available; seed 20261108 and the prefix grid [1,4,16,32] are fixed before the run, validation MSE alone selects 32 trees with no validation refit, bootstrap expanded row counts vary from 2,856 to 2,951, and leaf size counts multiplicity, not unique source rows. Out-of-bag excludes a row's entire exact-feature group, zero votes mean null, and prefixes 1/4/16/32 have 1,832/482/1/0 unsupported rows; each prefix's OOB MSE is on its own supported subset with a row-dependent number of trees, so it is not a fixed-set error curve or calibrated uncertainty interval, and a four-row duplicate witness demonstrates why row absence alone does not exclude an in-bag copy — which does not estimate real-data leakage magnitude. The 32-tree ensemble has test RMSE 0.705212 and MAE 0.545678 across 969 rows, versus same-fit least squares 0.734217/0.573791 and selected CART 0.775952/0.607582 — descriptive results on the reused historical test, not a fresh blind benchmark, statistical significance or causal isolation, and exact feature groups do not recover producer or batch identity. The complete ensemble JSON, all group draws and fitting/validation/test member outputs, per-row OOB votes and test predictions are saved, with 57 tests and a six-module/ten-CLI installed-wheel check covering full reproduction, independent traversal, group multiplicity, missing support, corruption guards and test-target mutation, and the original v0.1 data, models, report and audit figure unchanged. The protocol is fixed before held-out evaluation: the original UCI white-wine CSV is bundled without modification with its SHA-256, original names file and CC BY 4.0 attribution — 4,898 rows with 11 physicochemical features and an ordinal expert score, not a price, safety or causal target, and no official train/test split. Parsed feature values are serialized as binary64 float.hex strings, each vector hashed, the 3,961 unique groups sorted and the first floor(60%) assigned to fitting, the next through floor(80%) to validation and the rest to test — target-blind, source-row-order invariant and exact-feature-group disjoint, with target conflicts remaining together as a synthetic conflict control verifies, while near copies, unrecorded production batches and time-forward shift remain open. CART is fit with minimum leaf size 5 and maximum depth 20; greedy squared-error splits use every adjacent distinct feature boundary, left branches use <=, and weakest-link pruning saves every state's normalized fitting SSE, alpha, leaf count and removed node IDs, with exact floating ties cut together, ancestor cuts subsuming descendants and node statistics using math.fsum for reduction portability — the earlier dot-product prototype gave 307 states and the final stable statistics give 290 with the same selected predictions and test metrics. Validation MSE alone selects 19 leaves at alpha 0.00365165 with no refit; all 969 test predictions, the complete grown and selected model JSON and six first-test-row prediction routes are saved, the test is evaluated only after fitting, pruning and validation selection, and changing all test targets leaves the fitting, pruning path, validation scores, selected index and selected model unchanged. The control table keeps the stronger control in view: fitting mean 0.88943/0.67329, fitting median 0.90235/0.64499, grown CART with 425 leaves 0.88255/0.66363, validation-selected CART with 19 leaves 0.77595/0.60758 and ordinary least squares with 12 coefficients 0.73422/0.57379 (test RMSE/MAE), where the linear control uses fitting-only centering and scaling with an intercept solved by NumPy's least-squares routine and no tuned ridge parameter, squared error treats the ordinal scores as numeric, complexity units differ and this single historical split does not establish significance, future accuracy, a causal benefit of pruning or superiority over other model families. The independent checks are not self-agreement alone: forty all-threshold stump references recompute candidate SSEs directly, every subtree of 12 small depth-three fitted trees is enumerated with exact Fraction risks from training rows, production path and explicit-alpha prune objectives are checked at breakpoints, interval interiors, zero and a large alpha, and separate controls cover simultaneous weakest links, boundary inclusivity, adjacent binary64 threshold fallback, validation-score ties and target-scale alpha units, with full refit and report reproduction, all saved test predictions, group overlap, test-target mutation, linear normal equations, strict JSON corruption and complete SVG metadata, series and metrics checked, and the wheel installed outside the checkout exercising all five modules and all five CLI modes — demo, audit, fit, prune and predict — with prediction returning the complete branching route. The scope is stated plainly: greedy CART is not globally optimal tree learning, weakest-link pruning searches subtrees of one grown tree, alpha is score-squared per leaf because SSE is divided by the root fitting count, binary64 arithmetic, finite oracles and exact floating tie rules are not exact all-input certificates, a route explains this model's computation rather than feature causality or deletion effects, the original single-tree v0.1 has no missing or categorical data, sample weights, ensembles or production service, and out-of-range predictions are piecewise constant with no accuracy promise. Data and derived figures and metrics are Cortez et al. (2009), UCI Wine Quality, DOI 10.24432/C56S3T, CC BY 4.0; code is MIT; CART, weakest-link pruning and least squares are established methods, not claimed inventions, with no endorsement implied. The complete frozen ensemble is bundled as deterministic `bagging-model.json.gz` (gzip of the full JSON, zero timestamp) and CLI model loading accepts `.json.gz` as well as plain JSON — storage only, not trees, draws or predictions.

- **DigitLab** - Real UCI digit training in NumPy with manual gradients and a frozen official-test audit, built from scratch — it keeps the honest baseline in view: on 1,797 test digits, exact 1NN gets 1,760 correct, the tanh MLP (2,410 parameters) 1,734 and linear softmax (650 parameters) 1,698, all using the same 3,062 fitting examples, with test NLL 0.12096 and 0.16906 for the two trained models, so a stronger same-fit baseline is the result to beat, not a footnote. At normalized L-infinity radius 0.1, MLP correct counts fall to 898 under FGSM and 768 under a 20-step/three-start PGD candidate pool, versus 1,708 under one seeded random-sign control — continuous input attacks, not imperceptibility, certified robustness or an adaptive 1NN ranking. The v0.5 extension adds baseline-relative class-logit Integrated Gradients to a live model inspector for the unchanged UCI digit models: choose the class and a zero or fit-only mean reference, inspect all 64 signed contributions and their logit-sum residual, and keep the attack label separate. An algebraic tanh specialization is checked against independent 128-node Gauss-Legendre quadrature on 40 reference vectors, with maximum coordinate difference 3.56e-15 and maximum sum residual 1.07e-14 across all 7,188 official model/reference/row cases — finite numerical checks, not exact proofs. Both references are explicit — all-zero block counts and the frozen mean of only the 3,062 fitting rows, never validation or test rows — and the explained quantity is a selected class logit, not a softmax probability; neither reference is presumed neutral, realistic or causally meaningful. The v0.4 inspector runs manual JavaScript inference and input gradients with no upload or prediction service: paint with mouse, touch or keyboard, inspect all ten scores and compute clipped FGSM, with editing clearing the attack label, and a real browser checks 320/390/1280px layout, keyboard painting, touch dragging, both models, attack bounds, clear and restore. The v0.3 extension solves all 1,797 clipped adversarial-radius infima exactly — 99 clean errors, 796 positive attained thresholds and 902 positive unattained infima, with lowest-index ties deciding whether the boundary itself is an error and all 7,188 old box decisions agreeing; the first attained example is row 6 (true 6, competitor 4) and the first unattained is row 0 (true 0, competitor 9). The v0.2 extension adds exact real affine-box correctness for the fixed linear model — 1,698 / 1,495 / 1,028 / 23 of 1,797 test rows at radii 0 / 1/20 / 1/10 / 1/5 — with attained witnesses exposing 14 and 10 saved PGD survivors at the latter two radii, and all 64,692 margins and 7,188 witness decisions retained. The test counts grow with the versions: 30 tests in v0.1 including 201 independent gradient probes, full refit without test-loader access and saved-report reproduction; 45 in v0.2 including 144 all-vertex certificates and 160 independent Fraction row references; 59 in v0.3 including two independent 144-case vertex references, 160 Fraction row references, 100 official-boundary checks and three full-report figures; 64 Python tests and four Node/real-Chrome tests in v0.4 with 52 independent NumPy reference cases; 78 Python tests and six Node/real-Chrome tests in v0.5 with 106 attribution reference vectors plus the existing 52 inference/gradient cases, four reproducible audit figures and ten-module/ten-CLI wheel checks. The protocol is fixed and disclosed: UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits, not MNIST — 64 block counts in 0..16, normalized by 16 — with the original 3,823-row training file hash-partitioned per class into 3,062 fitting and 761 validation rows, the 1,797 official test rows kept separate, both models trained 80 epochs by seeded shuffled minibatch SGD with momentum and selected by minimizing validation NLL (both select epoch 80), and test examples parsed only after model selection. The scope is one historical benchmark and a fixed protocol, not superiority or SOTA claims; 1NN getting 1,678 correct on the MLP's epsilon-0.1 PGD images is transfer, not an adaptive attack on 1NN, and surviving these attacks is not proof of robustness. Original data, models and reports are preserved with CC BY 4.0 attribution.
