- **ScanLab** - Exact affine prefix scans and input-gated state-space derivatives, built from scratch in pure Python — it keeps algorithmic span separate from real arithmetic work and from floating behavior, on supplied tiny affine tables where every state, gradient and count is an exact rational, not a claim about speed or trained-model quality. Diagonal affine pairs compose in temporal order; a reduction/reconstruction inclusive scan retains every operand, interval, pair and dependency depth. A fixed diagonal LTI recurrence equals direct causal convolution, with the nonzero-initial-state boundary term and skip connection explicit, and a separate sigmoid input-gated recurrence exposes every forward state and hand-derived parameter/input/initial-state adjoint, with streaming carrying state across chunk boundaries without changing causal outputs. The failure controls are the point: a floating cancellation control writes [1e16,1,-1e16,1] with decay 1, where the sequential final state is 1, the scan is 0 and the exact rational result is 2; and an input-gate control gives final f(2x)-2f(x) about 0.20243342, so one fixed linear convolution cannot represent that model on all inputs — neither failing control is turned into a tolerance-based success. The exact 16-token, two-state fixture uses 26 pair compositions: unit vector composition/apply span is 7 versus recurrence span 16, while scalar multiplications rise from 32 to 136 and additions from 32 to 84 — actual DAG counts on supplied pairs, not runtime, GPU/FLOPs or fraction bit complexity, and both paths execute serial Python. The v0.3 extension couples dense states: full rational transition matrices mix state coordinates, matrix-affine composition preserves temporal multiplication order, and reverse propagation uses the next transposed matrix, exposing every state JVP, full matrix/write/initial VJP, outer-product gradient and primal/tangent/reverse DAG. The exact transposition identity is -440563/15120 on both sides; omitting only the transpose in reverse propagation gives gap 7831/2520; reversing only matrix-product order, with correct write composition, changes final state [9/2,187/36] to [-14/9,2/3] — and both mistakes become invisible under diagonal restrictions, which are tested separately, while singular reset steps work without inverses. Dense composition costs cubic work in state width: the six-token, two-state fixture uses seven matrix-matrix products and thirteen matrix-vector products, 108 multiplications/80 additions versus sequential 24/24, with unit-pair span 4 versus 6 treating whole matrix operations as units, not hardware latency; Fifty-one instrumented rational cases count actual arithmetic calls, excluding tangent construction, gradient products, loss reductions and bit complexity, so they are not end-to-end derivative costs. The v0.2 extension records forward/tangent/reverse/loss-reverse/HVP-reverse scan DAGs with every parameter VJP and every state JVP, and hand-written forward-over-reverse rules compute squared-loss gradient and HVP without dense matrices: the exact transposition identity is -817/120 on both sides, a wrong reverse-index control using the current rather than next decay gives 139/360 with gap -259/36, fixture loss is 2473/288, first directional derivative 83689/17280 and second 20591177/414720, and an independent bivariate polynomial expansion checks 432 parameter coordinates across 36 models, including each VJP, gradient and HVP coordinate, with another 24 directional models, 24 Hessian symmetry checks and 51 independent future-decay product models. The v0.3 suite has 63 tests, preserving all 46 earlier tests and saved artifacts and adding 96 independent homogeneous-product shapes, full DAGs through length 32, 216 associativity cases, 672 full-polynomial VJP parameter coordinates across 36 models, 24 full-direction polynomial models and 27 diagonal restrictions, with three reproducible full-witness SVGs and six-command/nine-module installed wheel parity; the v0.2 increment had 46 tests including the original 30, with a new 68-cell figure embedding all five complete DAGs, the original 114-cell figure preserved, and five CI jobs including Python 3.10-3.13 and all four CLI commands from an installed eight-module wheel outside checkout; the original 30 tests include 729 scalar composition cases, 264 length/width cases against an independent product expansion, 120 fixed LTI models, 123 independent gradient probes, all 64 streaming partitions, full DAG/count checks and strict invalid inputs, with the 16 displayed gradient probes having maximum central-difference error about 8.419e-12. The scope is supplied tiny affine tables only — width 1..8 and length 0..128 in v0.1, width 1..4 and length 0..32 on the new dense path — with rational algebra and float sigmoid arithmetic as separate paths, finite differences validating fixtures rather than all derivatives, and no dense HVP, general AD engine, symbolic compiler, new scan algorithm, learned-task quality, general long-context memory, GPU speed, FFT, HiPPO or Mamba/S4 claim; seven prior sources are attributed in v0.1 and six sources in each of the v0.2 and v0.3 derivations, with the full equations, counters and independent tests in the source NOTES.md, ADJOINT.md and DENSE.md.

- **Correlab** - Exact-rational audits of supplied finite general-sum joint play, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a joint law with no profitable fixed-action deviation and a joint law that still tells a player to replace their recommended action inspectable, on supplied small games where every gain, contribution and attaining deviation is an exact rational, not a claim about real-agent strength. A single regret number can hide that distinction, the probability of the signal, or the effect of removing correlation, so Correlab separates coarse fixed-action deviations, weighted one-pair internal deviations, unrestricted recommendation-dependent swap maps and conditional incentives, and it does not search for an equilibrium or train an agent. Every deviation retains all opponent-action probabilities, payoff differences and exact contributions; each fixed deviation retains recommendation-wise gains, and full-swap optimization separates by recommendation — choose an action maximizing each row of weighted gains, then add the maxima — with the report retaining every optimal choice, the number of optimal maps, the lexicographically first attaining map and all its joint-cell contributions. Conditional gains divide by the recommendation's positive probability and zero-probability recommendations return null, each player's gains stay separate, and the report also retains marginals, their product, exact product equality, total variation and an independently audited product control — only a product law that is CCE is labeled a Nash product. The controls that expose misleading summaries are the point: Roth's explicitly attributed lecture-8 CCE-not-CE game has uniform diagonal play with expected payoff 3/10, best raw fixed gain -3/10 and clipped coarse epsilon zero, yet recommendation C permits conditional gain 11/10 and weighted and full-swap gain 11/30; a developed coordination law pays 3/2 per player and is CE while its product-marginals control pays 3/4 with swap regret 1/4, so identical marginals do not imply identical joint laws or incentives; a rare recommendation has mass 1/1000 and weighted gain 1/1000 while its conditional incentive is one; and three equally likely recommendations with row payoffs [0,0,1] have largest one-pair gain 1/3 but full-swap gain 2/3, with an attaining map sending all recommendations to action 2. Realized joint actions produce exact prefix count laws, and independent sums of actual, fixed-action counterfactual and attaining-swap counterfactual payoffs certify that total regret equals prefix length times the corresponding empirical-law gain; final empirical reports are order-invariant while prefixes and last-round laws need not be, and the developed six-round history finishes with an empirical CCE while the final action pair has coarse epsilon 11/10 — a finite arithmetic identity and counterexample, not an implemented learner or no-regret guarantee. The v0.2 extension asks what the player observes: a trusted executor retains the hidden baseline action, the player sees only a supplied private signal and may pass or override with the opponent left at baseline — an explicit unilateral decision model, not CE of a changed game. Every signal/action retains exact joint-cell contributions, conditional gains, zero-mass nulls, all optimal choices and an attaining policy; identity signals recover full-swap gain and identical-row channels recover clipped coarse gain, and a supplied post-garbling retains both audits plus a lifted optimal coarse policy with exact nonnegative per-signal slacks summing to information loss. All deterministic own-action partitions through four actions are audited independently for each player with every strict refinement relation — the Bell counts are 1, 2, 5, 15 — and a developed cyclic control gives row gains 0, 4/3, 5/3, 1, 2 for partitions 000, 001, 010, 011, 012, showing three two-signal partitions can differ, while the attributed Roth control yields 0, 11/120, 11/60, 11/30 as C's reveal probability rises 0, 1/4, 1/2, 1. The v0.2 suite has 50 tests, including independent enumeration for 8,505 channel audits, 28 larger/rectangular four-signal controls up to 625 policies per player, 243 supplied post-garbling lift certificates and all 16 signal-alphabet size pairs checked by direct lift simulation; three new SVG tests bind 25 numeric cells, complete report metadata and hidden lift-term tampering, all seven complete CLI outputs match an installed seven-module wheel outside checkout, the original three CLI outputs and old SVG are unchanged, and six additional source notes distinguish single-agent value from strategic welfare. The original v0.1 verification has 33 tests covering all 81 ternary 2x2 row tables against all 35 quarter-grid joint laws with a separately varied column table — 2,835 game/law cases checked by exhaustive action-map enumeration — plus 28 rectangular/larger controls enumerating up to 256 maps per player, all 256 length-four 2x2 histories at three prefixes, and three SVG tests binding all 36 numeric cells, the full compressed report, hidden-term tampering and exact regeneration, with the six-module installed wheel matching all three full CLI outputs outside checkout and CI on Python 3.10-3.13 plus a package-parity job. Two reproducible SVGs. The scope is supplied exact payoff tables and joint laws only — one to four actions per side, at most 1,000 realized rounds and 100 explicit checkpoints — with no simultaneous override outcome, global stochastic-channel optimization, universal Blackwell-order search, privacy, learning or agent safety claim, and no scalable solver, adaptive algorithm, causal experiment, hidden-information/extensive-form game, real-agent benchmark or safety certification; epsilon values are payoff incentives, not distance to an equilibrium set or statistical confidence. The source DESIGN.md provides proofs, the complete schema and six consulted sources — Blum-Mansour, Bonn, Roth, Barakat, Charles University and Hart-Mas-Colell — crediting the adapted Roth fixture and recording cost/payoff orientation differences plus source-text typos rather than silently inheriting them.

- **GameLab** - Exact-rational zero-sum game audits with complete optimal faces, minimax witnesses and deterministic perturbation certificates, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a unique game value and a unique optimal strategy inspectable, on supplied small zero-sum tables where every vertex, gap and bound is an exact rational, not a claim about real-agent strength. GameLab enumerates every active basis of an exact-rational security LP — at most 70 per player for a 4x4 table — retaining rank, determinant, pivots, all slacks and violations for every singular, infeasible and feasible attempt, then merges duplicate bases so all optimal vertices describe the full optimal face; the row player maximizes payoff while the column player minimizes it by solving the negative-transpose game, whose internal value has the opposite sign. A deterministic equal average of distinct optimal vertices supplies one equilibrium with pure-action payoff, best-reply, probability, primal/dual and complementary-slackness witnesses — not a uniquely preferred or maximum-entropy policy — and coordinate extrema include attaining vertices whose ranges are not independent choices. A separate strategy audit returns both unilateral gains and both security losses under the sum, not half-sum, exploitability convention. The failure worth seeing is the duplicate-action continuum: rock-paper-scissors has value zero and one-third mixed policies, yet both fixed action-zero policies also yield payoff zero while their unilateral gains sum to two, so a zero observed payoff is not an equilibrium certificate; in a 4x2 game with duplicate rows the optimal row vertices [0,1/2,1/2,0] and [1/2,0,1/2,0] make every convex combination optimal, the fourth action is dominated, and the representative [1/4,1/4,1/2,0] preserves the face rather than pretending it is unique; a constant 2x3 table has every policy optimal, and a separate nonzero game independently checks value 3/2 with row policy [1/4,3/4]. Strategies can be discontinuous even when values are stable: a two-row/one-column payoff flip of 10^-12 changes the unique optimal row from [0,1] to [1,0] with unchanged value, and a separate near-bound control approaches the two-epsilon transfer-gap bound — the payoff-change audit retains both complete solutions, entry differences, every maximum-difference location and exact bound slacks, so with epsilon the maximum entry change the value changes by at most epsilon and the old equilibrium pair has new gap at most two epsilon. The shared-table interval extension computes the exact worst joint deviation gap over a supplied full Cartesian entrywise interval box for one fixed policy pair facing one unknown payoff table, not two independently selected adverse tables: for every pure reply pair it constructs the signed coefficient of every entry before maximizing, retains endpoint choices and contributions, and returns an attaining matrix with a direct best-reply audit, while also reporting each player’s separately maximized unilateral gain and the conservatism of their sum. In the developed shared-entry control only A[0,0] varies in [0,2], all other entries are one and both policies choose action zero; the row worst deviation requires entry zero and the column worst requires entry two, so the exact shared worst gap is one while their incompatible separate maxima sum to two, the game value stays one throughout, and the separate robust-security pair is an equilibrium for every allowed table. For a full 2x2 unit box with uniform fixed policies the shared worst gap is 1/2 versus a separate worst-regret sum of one, and for a one-action game with sole payoff [-50,50] the value-range width is 100 but every policy pair’s gap is zero — payoff uncertainty is not the same as incentive to deviate. Complete endpoint game solves certify the attainable game-value extrema v(L) and v(U); the lower-game row policy and upper-game column policy separately optimize worst security and guarantee pair gap at most v(U)-v(L), with a signed audit that can be tighter, though the construction does not claim to minimize worst joint gap over policies. The 53 tests cover all 81 ternary 2x2 matrices against an independent one-dimensional envelope oracle, independent permutation determinants and every basis residual on four controls, optimal-vertex cross-pairs and convex mixtures, coordinate attaining witnesses, all 81 grid strategy pairs, affine/permutation/negative-transpose invariances, perturbation cases, 4x4 capacity and strict input failures — plus the extension’s all 16 zero/one-width boxes on a nonconstant 2x2 base, each at nine fixed policy pairs, against independent exhaustive endpoint enumeration, every candidate support contribution and attainment, corner game values and robust security, shared-entry and singleton cancellation, affine, zero-width, rectangular and 4x4 controls, strict schemas and no mutation; SVG checks bind 23 cells, four range segments and eight endpoints, then 29 numbers in the interval figure, with complete compressed report metadata, hidden-basis and hidden-coefficient tampering and exact regeneration. Two reproducible SVGs, six-module wheel parity reproducing all six complete CLI JSON outputs outside checkout with the original 37 tests and four CLI outputs unchanged, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI plus a package-parity job. The scope is small supplied tables only — 1..4 actions per side, exact bounded inputs, no runtime dependency or network call — with no general-sum, hidden-information, extensive-form, correlated-equilibrium, learned-opponent, payoff-estimation, scalable-solver or new-algorithm claim, and developed numerical fixtures are not held-out tournaments or evidence of real-agent strength. The project implements established minimax, LP duality and polytope geometry, with six consulted sources, the full proof, schemas and orientation differences in the source DESIGN.md and box support-function proofs in INTERVALS.md, crediting Li-Ding-Floudas, Gorissen-den Hertog and Bertsimas-den Hertog-Pauphilet.

- **BetLab** - Exact sequential evidence for bounded paired model scores, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a valid anytime test and a ruined test quietly given a free restart inspectable, on disclosed small fixtures where every wealth update, crossing probability and confidence bracket is an exact rational, not a claim about a deployed monitor. For differences D_t in [-1,1] under the one-sided conditional null E[D_t | past] <= 0, any past-measurable bet b_t in [0,1] multiplies wealth by 1+b_t*D_t, and a fixed convex mixture of such wealth processes stays one, so Ville's inequality bounds the probability of ever reaching 1/alpha by alpha at any stopping time within the fixed test. Two valid constructions are exposed separately: a fixed mixture over bets [0,1/4,1/2,3/4,1] with equal positive weights by default, and a past-only heuristic clip(sum_previous_D / (1 + sum_previous_D_squared), 0, 1) that excludes the current observation — a separate test, not an extra component chosen after seeing results. The failure worth seeing is the invalid free restart: on the eight-round fair-coin null tree (255 internal histories, 256 equiprobable leaves) at alpha 1/4, the fixed mixture crosses threshold 4 with probability 5/64 and the past-only strategy 9/128, both with expected final and stopped wealth 1, while injecting a fresh unit before the next round whenever wealth hits zero crosses with probability 201/256, expected final wealth 9/2 and stopped wealth 423/128 — the chance of at least one run of two positive signs in eight fair flips — and the node audit exposes negative conditional-expectation slack after ruin, so it is a counterexample to free refills, not to optional stopping. The six-round negative-drift tree (positive-sign probability 1/3, conditional mean -1/3) crosses with probability 1/81 for both valid constructions and 281/729 for the invalid restart. A reversal sequence of eight +1 then twelve -1 first crosses threshold 4 at round 4 for both valid processes, then ends below 4; a separate 32-round paired fixture with baseline 1/2 and candidate 3/4 crosses threshold 20 at round 19 for the mixture and 17 for the past-only heuristic. The start-allocation extension replaces free refills with a prespecified alternative: allocate one initial unit across deterministic start times before observing the stream — at most 16 starts in 1..256, positive weights summing exactly to one, ruined components staying zero and unstarted components contributing their reserved weight — and the complete statistic is a fixed convex mixture, not the largest start-specific result. On the delayed-signal control (eight negative, eight positive, eight negative differences), bet one with starts 1, 9, 17 and equal weights first crosses threshold 4 at round 12, has wealth 257/3 including reserve 1/3 at round 16, and ends at zero, while the one-start control is permanently ruined at round one. The fair eight-round null audit with starts 1, 3, 5, 9, each weight 1/4, keeps the round-nine budget reserved at every leaf and crosses with probability 5/32 at alpha 1/4 with final and stopped expectations both 1, while the invalid free-refill control stays 201/256; the six-round negative-drift law gives 17/729 with expected final wealth 1261/2916 and expected stopped wealth 683/1458. The v0.3 confidence-sequence inversion extension adds a deliberately conservative bounded-mean bet family with factors 1+b*(D-m)/2 for each candidate mean m in [-1,1]; monotone wealth makes the accepted mean set an interval, and exact rational bisection retains component-level crossing/noncrossing witnesses and every midpoint comparison, with running intersections retaining earlier exclusions after reversal. With bet one and alpha 1/4, 24 repeated differences of 1/2 give boundary bracket [12487/32768,1561/4096] and conservative closed interval [12487/32768,1]; eight +1 then twelve -1 retain the earlier interval [1273/2048,1] even while the current-prefix inversion weakens; sixteen bisections bound running-boundary uncertainty by 1/32768. An eight-round sign law with positive probability 3/4 and constant conditional mean 1/2 enumerates all 256 paths: ever-exclusion and true-mean crossing probabilities are both 2187/16384, below alpha 1/4, with every conditional mixture slack zero and final/stopped expectations one. The 67 tests build on the original 34 — independent exhaustive product references for all 243 length-five ternary sequences, a separate 64-path crossing/expectation reference, exact fair and negative-drift trees, predictable-prefix checks, all 256 observations and 16 mixture components, zero wealth, equality at threshold, running p-bound monotonicity, a marginal-zero but conditionally invalid law, variable-depth/zero-difference controls, internal rational differences larger than input component caps, alias/cycle/scope/input guards, and all four CLI outputs and error paths — with the start-allocation extension adding direct suffix-product references, all 256 fair paths, every conditional expectation and full 256-observation/16-start/16-bet capacity, and the confidence extension adding exhaustive independent products/brackets/trials on 81 ternary streams, exact endpoint/midpoint ties, refinement, reversal, prefix-only behavior, all 256 coverage paths and full capacity; SVG checks cover all 63 plotted coordinates, all nine null-table values, 92 new-figure coordinates and seven cells, full compressed report metadata, hidden-witness tampering and byte-exact regeneration. Three reproducible SVGs, seven-module wheel parity reproducing all ten complete CLI JSON outputs outside checkout, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. The scope is small supplied laws and developed fixtures only — at most 64 observations, 16 fixed bets, 1..32 bisections and eight-round trees — with no unbounded-score method, learned optimal bet, automatic stream selection, multiple-test correction, drifting-mean or causal-effect estimate, or production monitoring service; a crossing is not a posterior probability, an effect-size estimate or proof that a particular model is generally better, and non-crossing is not evidence of equivalence. The betting mathematics follows Waudby-Smith and Ramdas, "Estimating means of bounded random variables by betting" (JRSSB 86(1), OUP article 7043257), with the Ville-inequality context in arXiv:2304.01163, the mean/variance e-process and discrete-mixture background in arXiv:2301.12480, and Ji Won Park's 2025 tutorial on estimation as betting; this is an original small implementation, not a new inference theorem.

- **PrivacyLab** - Exact finite-channel differential privacy with rational hockey-stick events, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a marginally private-looking release and a jointly private one inspectable, on disclosed small fixtures where every delta, factor and witness is an exact rational, not a claim about a deployed mechanism. PrivacyLab audits supplied finite probability tables against explicit neighboring-secret edges, computing the smallest delta at an exact multiplicative factor t=exp(epsilon) with every directed-pair event witness retained; factor 3 means epsilon ln(3), not epsilon 3. The failure worth seeing is the shared-coin control: with Z zero with probability 3/4, releases A=secret XOR Z and B=Z have pure factors 3 and 1 alone, but A XOR B recovers the secret exactly, so the joint release has delta 1 for every finite factor — marginals do not compose when randomness is shared. The developed table keeps every factor and delta exact: randomized response at truthful probability 3/4 has factor 3 with deltas 1/2, 0, 0 at factors 1, 3, 9; two independent releases factor 9 with 1/2, 3/8, 0; a vote with a fresh fair tie-break factor 3 with 1/2, 0, 0; parity only factor 1 with 0, 0, 0; the shared-coin joint infinite with 1, 1, 1. The adaptive extension unfolds full history-conditioned mechanism trees: fixed round factors multiply to T, a substochastic clipping kernel bounds the actual joint delta by a telescoping leaf-mass deficit, and the fair-branch control has actual/clipped delta 1/2 against uniform/additive bounds of 1, while a revealing but impossible history kills zero mass and the shared-coin joint is reproduced exactly by its true conditional laws. The factor-frontier extension computes the complete worst-neighbor delta profile for every finite factor, certifying each interval against every overlapping local piece: on the developed three-secret/four-output control with rows [1/16,5/16,1/8,1/2], [5/16,3/8,1/16,1/4], [1/8,5/16,3/16,3/8] and edges 0-1, 1-2, the profile is 5/8-5t/16 from 1 to 5/4, 5/16-t/16 from 5/4 to 5, and zero after 5, with the maximizing direction switching at 5/4 — absent from the likelihood-ratio grid {1,6/5,3/2,2,5/2,3,5} — where both directions tie at delta 15/64. Exact inverse delta budgets retain minimality witnesses or singular-support obstructions: delta 1/4 needs factor 6/5, 15/64 needs 5/4, 0 needs 5, a singular plateau of 1/2 makes target 1/4 unattainable at any finite factor, and a full-support control needs the finite factor 500000000000000000000000000000. The 63 tests cover the original 29 — 24 seeded channels against exhaustive all-16-event searches per directed pair at four factors, a 10^-100 singular support leak kept exact, 36 SVG cells — plus 14 adaptive behavior and 3 SVG checks (24 seeded three-round/three-secret trees, exhaustive all-256-event searches per directed pair, six rounds, 64 leaves, eight secrets, all 28 edges) and 14 frontier behavior and 3 SVG checks (32 seeded three-secret/four-output channels, exhaustive all-16-event references, inverse checks at four targets, ten new query cells). Three reproducible SVGs, seven-module wheel parity reproducing all six complete CLI JSON outputs outside checkout, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. The scope is small supplied tables only — at most 8 secrets, 64 outputs and 28 undirected edges in the point audit, four secrets and eight outputs in the frontier, six rounds in the adaptive trees — with no trained-model certification, secure sampler, real-data adjacency proof, DP-SGD accountant, advanced composition or production privacy guarantee. Established DP and hockey-stick arithmetic follows Dwork and Roth with modern accounting notes; this is an original small implementation, not a new privacy theorem or production accountant.

- **SparseLab** - LASSO optimization audits with exact primal/dual/KKT witnesses, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a sparse-looking coefficient vector and an identified one inspectable, on disclosed small fixtures where every gap, KKT residual and branch is an exact rational or converged numerical reference, not a claim about held-out accuracy. Dependency-free cyclic coordinate descent minimizes `||y-X beta||^2/(2N) + lambda*||beta||_1` with every column penalized and no hidden intercept, standardization, centering or ridge term; every sweep reports primal loss/penalty, an interior scaled-residual feasible dual witness, all correlations, a stable gap decomposition, direct primal-minus-dual difference, all KKT residuals and support, and both absolute gap and maximum KKT residual must reach tolerance or the run is an explicit nonconvergence result. The solver is checked against closed form first: on a four-row orthogonal design over six dyadic penalties, the coefficients match the closed-form soft thresholds of `[2,-3/4,1/8]` with lambda_max=2 — a mathematical control, not a held-out accuracy claim. The ambiguity controls are the point: on duplicate columns at lambda=1/4 the forward fit `[7/4,0,-1/2]`, reverse fit `[0,7/4,-1/2]` and midpoint `[7/8,7/8,-1/2]` all reach objective `5/8` with identical predictions and zero KKT residual, so unique predictions do not identify coefficients or a causal selected feature; on correlated columns at lambda=1/8 one sweep leaves gap `0.330882353` and max KKT `0.176470588`, 18 sweeps reach objective `19/68`, and the exact rational active/sign enumerator independently agrees with `[0,33/17]`. The Gap Safe extension audits any supplied iterate with exact rational arithmetic over the stored binary64 inputs: a positive exact margin `lambda-|X_j^T u|` with `||X_j||^2*R_squared < margin^2` proves coefficient j zero in every optimizer — a near iterate with current beta_2=1/32 has gap `5/1024` and certifies feature 2 (ball term `5/512` < squared margin `25/1024`, slack `15/1024`), a loose iterate with a zero column has gap `73/32` and certifies it anyway, while an exact boundary zero has radius=margin=0 and stays uncertified, a duplicate-column optimum screens neither copy, and an equality control at ball=squared margin=1/4 stays uncertified — a sufficient certificate, not a complete support detector. The affine-branch extension verifies the drop/re-entry phenomenon with exact KKT branches instead of a dense grid of approximate fits: on the developed centered four-row fixture with columns a and 2a+b, response 2a-b, Gram `[[1,2],[2,5]]` and cross `[2,3]`, five supplied branches cover `[3,infinity)`, `[4/3,3]`, `[1,4/3]`, `[1/3,1]` and `[0,1/3]` — feature 1 enters positive at 3, feature 0 enters at 4/3, feature 1 leaves at 1 and re-enters negative at 1/3, adjacent knots agree exactly in coefficients, predictions, objectives and dual vectors, and the zero-penalty endpoint is `[4,-1]`; selected signs are weak, so boundary zeros are allowed and an infeasible pattern returns an explicit noncertificate rather than a fabricated answer. The 62 tests cover the original 28 — independent scalar/orthogonal formulas, ten seeded exact-rational comparisons, full five-column enumeration, duplicate convex mixtures, weak duality, all primal/dual equations, monotone sweep histories, warm/cold agreement, explicit path failure propagation, symmetries, row replication, scale/penalty equivalence, zero columns, strict malformed JSON, nonmutation and RNG isolation, with three SVG checks over all 18 coefficient plot points, 23 table cells and byte-exact regeneration — plus 14 behavior and 3 SVG checks for the Gap Safe extension (24 seeded designs against independent active/sign-oracle witnesses, exact dual-optimum containment, 27 SVG cells) and 14 behavior and 3 SVG checks for the branch extension (12 seeded five-row/three-feature designs exercising all 27 sign patterns, 20 figure cells, both path geometries with all 10 vertices, five knots, hidden singular-Gram tampering). Three reproducible SVGs, seven-module wheel parity reproducing all six complete CLI JSON outputs outside checkout with all three SVGs regenerating byte-exactly, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. The scope is small dense fits only — at most 128 rows and 12 columns, exact oracle at most 32 rows and 5 columns, positive lambda — with floating-point dual/KKT checks that are not outward-rounded certificates and no coefficient error bounds in rank-deficient problems; no held-out accuracy, cross-validation, causal feature importance or post-selection inference. Standard coordinate descent and soft thresholding follow Friedman, Hastie and Tibshirani, KKT and dual geometry follow Tibshirani's coordinate-descent notes, Gap Safe rules follow Fercoq, Gramfort and Salmon, and piecewise-linear LASSO paths follow Efron, Hastie, Johnstone and Tibshirani with Rosset and Zhu — this is an original small implementation with developed controls, not a new algorithm, a general LARS solver, a complete support-recovery method or a speed benchmark.

- **InfluenceLab** - Convex logistic influence functions with converged Newton fits and full Hessian/solve witnesses, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the gap between a local weight derivative and an actual row deletion inspectable, on a disclosed seven-row fixture where every derivative, refit and deletion path is a converged numerical reference, not symbolic arithmetic. InfluenceLab fits small fully regularized binary logistic models with Newton steps, unjittered Cholesky solves, Armijo backtracking and an explicit numerical convergence requirement, reporting the full Hessian, Cholesky factors, parameter directions, query-loss derivatives, adjoint agreement, solve residuals and all refit histories; there is no implicit intercept, and a supplied constant column is regularized too. Seven synthetic rows, query (1,2) with label 1, ridge 0.1 and original normalizer 7 give theta approximately (-0.275488,0.220249) and query loss 0.614042. For row 5 the linear fixed-N deletion prediction is +0.203204 while the actual fixed-N numerical refit is +0.305655, a linear-minus-refit error of -0.102451; the retained-row mean with the same ridge gives +0.317231 and a damped inverse with delta 0.5 gives +0.119499, so neither shortcut equals the refit — fixed-N deletion equals retained-mean deletion only with ridge multiplied by N/(N-1), not with unchanged ridge, and adding 0.5 to the inverse Hessian is not refitting a ridge-0.6 model. All 15 base/deletion numerical fits have max absolute gradient at most 8.197e-11 and undamped influence solves have max residual 5.551e-17; residuals measure numerical equations, not statistical uncertainty or deletion accuracy. The `trace_deletion` extension continuously lowers selected weights from 1 to 0 at 17 grid nodes, refitting from zero at each node and checking integrated local derivatives against actual endpoint loss across 51 fits in three groups: row 5 has Simpson16 delta +0.305655349 against an endpoint +0.305655, residual +1.381e-7; rows 5 and 6 jointly have Simpson16 -0.357502140 against an endpoint -0.357507, residual +4.825e-6, while their separate finite-deletion changes sum to only -0.142898, so group deletion is not a sum of separate refits even though the group derivative at the base point is the sum of individual derivatives; deleting all seven rows reverses the frozen first-order sign, -0.004253 against an actual +0.079105, with Simpson16 +0.079849431 leaving a visible residual of +7.442e-4 from the sharp derivative tail, and the all-row endpoint has theta zero and query loss log(2). A small successive quadrature difference is not an error certificate, and monotone error reduction is not promised for arbitrary inputs. The 43 tests cover the original 26 — scalar fit against a separate monotone bisection optimizer, primitive and full-objective finite differences, every parameter weight derivative against perturbed converged refits on the fixture and three seeded systems up to six parameters, independent 2D inverse and adjoint solves, curvature sum-of-squares, strong-convexity error diagnostics, actual deletion and normalization equivalence, local quadratic remainder behavior, orthogonal-coordinate/permutation/label-complement checks, duplicate/zero features, explicit nonconvergence and safeguarded backtracking, strict JSON and stable extreme logits, with three of them inspecting every chart cell/position and the complete compressed JSON metadata including hidden refit-Hessian tampering — plus 14 new behavior tests for the path extension (quadrature against independent polynomial integrals, interior parameter/query derivatives against perturbed refits, all-node equations and convergence, local sum identities, direct grouped endpoints, sign reversal/nonadditivity, grid fit reuse, symmetry and strict API/CLI) and three SVG tests checking summary/convergence cells, all 51 polyline points, full metadata and hidden interior-Hessian tampering. Two reproducible SVGs, five-module wheel parity reproducing all four complete JSON reports outside checkout with both SVGs regenerating byte-exactly, and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. The scope is converged floating-point references on small positive-ridge convex logistic models: no causal attribution, bad-label detection guarantee, privacy deletion, machine-unlearning guarantee, neural-network scalability or confidence interval, and no paid APIs, runtime dependencies or private datasets. Standard influence-function mathematics is credited to Koh and Liang (2017) Section 2.1 for the inverse-Hessian derivative — this API uses unit sample weights inside the original-N average, hence the explicit 1/N — with Basu, Pope and Feizi noted for deep-model fragility this project does not reproduce; the fixture was developed during implementation, and this is an original small implementation, not a new explanation algorithm.

- **FisherLab** - Exact-rational natural-policy-gradient geometry, built from scratch in pure Python with no runtime dependencies — it makes the qualifiers on "parameterization invariant" executable, on a disclosed three-action fixture where every Fisher matrix, nullspace and finite-step control is computed exactly. Three actions have probabilities (1/2,1/3,1/6) and rewards (0,1,3), so expected reward is 5/6 and reward variance is 41/36; four full-tangent charts — baseline rows (1,0),(0,1),(0,0), shear rows (1,2),(0,1),(0,0), full logits, and a redundant four-parameter chart — give exactly the same natural probability tangent (-5/12,1/18,13/36), with natural L1 gap 0 in every chart, while diagonal approximations miss by 5/9, 14/15, 31/60 and 37/20 and coordinate damping G+(1/10)I misses by 7/17, 43/213, 63/278 and 31/216, so the gaps are L1 distances to the full-simplex natural tangent, not performance scores, and these particular differences demonstrate non-invariance rather than a universal ranking of approximation methods. A restricted one-parameter chart has rank 1 and gaps 4/9, 4/9 and 11/21, and its tangent (-5/12,5/18,5/36) is the exact Fisher-metric projection into its smaller reachable space, not a failed solver. The finite-step trap is separate: a binary model with p(action1)=sigmoid(z), rewards (0,1) and local coordinate z=theta^3 at theta=1 has natural directions dz=1 and dtheta=1/3, so the initial mapped logit tangent agrees exactly, but at step eta=1/2 Euler updates give logit 3/2 directly versus 343/216 in the curved chart, an exact difference of 19/216, with updated action-1 probabilities approximately 0.817574 versus 0.830329; and a step of 1 along the exact logit natural direction on the three-action fixture has actual KL(p_old || p_new)=0.725586 against a local quadratic prediction of 41/72=0.569444, with a linear reward prediction of 71/36=1.972222 against an actual 2.303238 — local quantities are not exact finite-step constraints or promises. The shared-context extension aggregates exact Fisher and reward-gradient systems across 1-6 fixed contexts sharing parameters: with context frequencies (3/4,1/4), equal binary action probabilities and rewards (0,1) and (0,-2), a shared parameter has natural direction 1/4, total reward derivative positive 1/64, but the second context's derivative is negative -1/8, and the full-conditional target has exact squared length 7/16 = 1/64 + 27/64, reachable plus metric-orthogonal residual, while independent context parameters reach both targets with zero residual as a larger model, not a training benchmark; replacing frequencies with uniform weights changes the objective, its natural direction -1/2 having derivative -1/32 on the original weighted objective, and a separate context-gauge control is frozen under conditional normalization but has spurious nonzero Fisher and gradient if naively modeled as a global joint softmax. Output carries the logit Fisher, probability Jacobian, pullback Fisher, per-action score rows, two independent-form Gram reconstructions, a positive weighted pairwise outer-product PSD certificate, gradient, full RREF augmented matrix, pivot and free columns, all nullspace directions, solve residual and induced null tangents, with per-direction parameter, logit and probability vectors, objective derivative, Fisher length, probability-space length and L1 gap; the exact RREF representative sets free variables to zero and is not a Moore-Penrose pseudoinverse or minimum-Euclidean-norm solution, and for full charts the natural objective derivative and both metric lengths equal reward variance, while restricted charts report rank and projection gap explicitly. Inputs are 2-8 strictly positive rational probabilities summing exactly to 1, aligned rational rewards, 1-12 coordinate columns and positive rational damping, with integers, Fractions and rational or decimal strings accepted, floats and booleans rejected, rational inputs capped at 128 numerator/denominator bits and strings at 100 characters, and the CLI requiring exactly version 1, probabilities, rewards, coordinates and damping, rejecting duplicate and unknown fields and files over 100KB, with invalid calls exiting 2 without a JSON result. The 45 tests cover the original 28 — 25 behavior tests including independently computed finite-difference reward and probability derivatives, every entry of a KL Hessian, exact closed forms, 16 seeded chart configurations up to 8 actions and 12 parameters, gauge, reward-shift and ordering invariance, frozen, restricted and overcomplete charts, complete singular-system residual and null witnesses, sum-of-squares quadratic certificates, finite-step controls, strict API and CLI errors, immutability and global RNG isolation, plus three SVG tests verifying all 60 table cells and positions, exact nonlinear logit controls, complete compressed report metadata, hidden witness tampering and byte regeneration — and 17 more for the context extension, 14 behavior plus three SVG, including independent weighted reward and KL finite differences, centered joint-score reconstruction, exact projection and null certificates, strict CLI and conditional-versus-joint controls. Two reproducible SVGs and dependency-free Python 3.10-3.13 CI running both CLI paths, SVG regeneration and installed-wheel comparisons outside the source checkout, with the demo asserting both agreement and counterexamples and the committed full report, strict input, proof and scope document and byte-reproducible SVG making the numbers inspectable. Standard natural-gradient and Fisher ideas are credited to Kakade (2001) Section 2 and Martens (2020) Sections 5 and 9 on Fisher and pullback, Section 10 on damping and Section 12 on infinitesimal versus finite-step parameterization invariance — but this is an original small-model implementation, exact witnesses, controls and test suite, not a new algorithm or a reproduction of published training experiments.

- **LatentLab** - A trained Gaussian variational autoencoder built from scratch in scalar Python — it makes the gap between reconstructing well and generating well inspectable, on a disclosed synthetic fixture where every gradient, evidence estimate and failure control is computed exactly or audited. The 150-parameter model uses 12-unit tanh hidden layers in both encoder and decoder, a diagonal posterior with smooth bounded logvariance, and a Gaussian decoder likelihood with fixed standard deviation 0.2; reparameterized latent samples and analytic Gaussian KL feed a hand-derived backward pass through every weight, bias and logvariance path, with Adam bias correction, and a strict versioned JSON checkpoint supports detached export/import, reproducible inference and prior sampling. Both arms train on the same 128 synthetic eight-mode points from identical initialized weights and data/noise RNG streams for 800 minibatch Adam updates, with neither seed nor checkpoint selected on held-out outcomes, and another 128 points are used for evaluation; negative ELBO uses four held-fixed posterior-noise draws per point with analytic KL, and prior generation includes independent observation-likelihood noise, not only decoded means. The held-out table makes the point concrete: negative ELBO 13.5149 untrained, 8.0758 at beta 0, 1.6600 at beta 1; analytic posterior KL 0.3324, 9.3704, 2.2073; posterior-mean MSE per coordinate 0.44105, 0.00268, 0.00281; prior-sample sliced W2 squared 0.1138, 0.0676, 0.0241; and 83.59%, 71.09%, 51.56% of prior samples outside radius 0.3 of all mode centers — the no-KL arm has slightly lower deterministic reconstruction error yet worse prior-sample distance, beta 1 improves the measured prior samples but over half still lie outside the disclosed neighborhoods, and the observation noise 0.2 is wider than the fixture jitter 0.06, so the distance diagnostic also reflects likelihood-family mismatch; sliced transport uses 32 deterministic directions and radius 0.3 is a disclosed synthetic diagnostic, not calibrated support estimation. The importance extension keeps the VAE unchanged and adds a separate inference audit with complete importance witnesses — latent draws, joint/proposal log densities, normalized weights, log mean weight, mean log weight and ESS — returning the analytic-KL MC negative ELBO separately because it is not the same finite-sample quantity as negative mean log weight, and only expectation bounds carry the IWAE monotonic hierarchy, so one random prefix need not improve. For the trained beta 1 anchor x=(1,0), shared Gaussian-noise prefixes of size 1, 4, 16 and 64 give log-evidence estimates -2.0464, 0.7106, -0.2701 and -0.9271, visibly nonmonotone, while the true nonlinear evidence is not known and no finite random row is guaranteed to bound it. A separate linear-Gaussian model has exact log evidence -2.15379530672424, verified by four independent Simpson-integral reference settings, and with its exact posterior as proposal all weights are constant and the 64-draw estimate recovers that value. The two-atom failure control is stronger than a seed-picked bad run: joint masses (0.000001, 0.999999) against proposal masses (0.999, 0.001) with true evidence 1, so with probability (999/1000)^64 = 93.79749638258456% all 64 draws hit the common proposal atom, ESS is then 64, but the log estimate is -13.814510 rather than 0, an underestimate by factor 999,000 — full support is present and nearly all joint mass is simply unseen, so high ESS only measures observed weight balance, not missed mass. Finite binomial enumeration with exact rational event probabilities gives expected evidence exactly 1 for every K, while expected log estimates for K=1, 4, 16, 64 are about -13.793788, -13.737278, -13.529377 and -12.785808: they rise and stay below 0 while remaining far from the truth. The 43 tests cover the original 25 — independent central differences of every parameter in nine hidden-size/beta settings, a fixed-noise batch derivative, saturated logvariance derivatives, zero-model analytic loss, exact-prior KL, an independently calculated first Adam update, strict checkpoint validation, immutable data, no global RNG effects, deterministic training and CLI success/failure paths, with the unchanged zero-step model deliberately failing the improvement gate and a collapsed origin cloud failing the disclosed distribution diagnostic — plus 15 importance tests and three figure tests, including exhaustive binary IID sequences through K=8, independent normal-density products, extreme-log underflow reports, exact-posterior constants, expected ELBO/posterior-KL identities, nonmutating inference, strict CLI validation and the real neural prefix fixture; three visual tests check all 12 metric lines, all 1,536 scatter coordinates and common axes, byte reproduction, complete compressed-report metadata and tampering with an undisplayed checkpoint weight, and the new figure tests check all 48 numeric cells/positions, complete witness metadata and an undisplayed log-joint change. Two reproducible SVGs and a five-module v0.2 wheel that reproduces both demos outside checkout, with original demo output and original SVG byte-identical, dependency-free Python 3.10-3.13 CI that runs the actual training demo, SVG check and wheel-installed demo outside checkout, with installed and source runs compared as complete JSON objects. Standard variational autoencoding, reparameterization and analytic Gaussian KL are credited to Kingma & Welling, and standard importance/IWAE formulas to Burda, Grosse & Salakhutdinov — but this is an original scalar implementation, synthetic fixture, audit and test suite, not a new algorithm or a reproduction of their experiments, and it is inference auditing, not IWAE training, a confidence interval, a new estimator, an ESS accuracy certificate or exact neural evidence: only beta 1 is the ordinary negative-ELBO objective, the posterior is diagonal with bounded variance, output noise is fixed, and there is only one two-dimensional data fixture and default seed, with no posterior-collapse, disentanglement, image-generation or generalization claim.

### TreeLab: the control table shares one fitting set

[Source](https://github.com/midya8/treelab)

The entry quotes the control table's five numbers and never says what they share. Fitting mean 0.88943, fitting median 0.90235, grown CART with 425 leaves 0.88255, validation-selected CART with 19 leaves 0.77595, ordinary least squares with 12 coefficients 0.73422 — test RMSE, with MAE 0.67329, 0.64499, 0.66363, 0.60758 and 0.57379 alongside — and every one of those five models was fit on the same 2,921 fitting rows. That shared denominator is the whole point of the table: the numbers are comparable because the fit is identical, not because five models happened to land near each other.

Those 2,921 rows are the fitting split the entry already describes in groups. The 4,898-row table collapses to 3,961 unique exact-feature groups, and the first floor(60%) of them — 2,376 groups — go to fitting. The table header states the row count those groups amount to; the entry carried the groups and left the rows out.

Distinct from 'A row left out is not a row held out', TreeLab's principle about the granularity at which a row counts as excluded — here nothing is being excluded, the question is what the five compared models have in common — and from 'A search that runs out of budget owes you the gap', which is about an unfinished search reporting the gap it left — here every model finished, and the shared fit is what licenses the comparison at all.

None of this changes the ranking. The entry covers the table, the linear control's fitting-only centering and scaling, and the limits — one historical split, no significance, complexity units differ. The 2,921 rows are the denominator underneath: the reason the table can say the stronger control is stronger, on this fit, on this split, and no further.

Case study: [treelab.md](treelab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/latentlab](https://github.com/midya8/latentlab)

- **ErgodLab** - Exact stationary policy sensitivities, independently checked against directed spanning trees, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a one-step quantity and a long-run quantity inspectable, on supplied finite models where every stationary distribution, derivative and variance can be computed exactly. The anchored policy is proportional to `weight(s,a)*exp(feature(s,a) dot theta)`, with fixed transitions and rewards and theta evaluated at zero; exact pivoted linear solves return stationary distributions and derivatives, and a structurally independent directed spanning-tree product/quotient rule returns the same values and every derivative. The occupancy fixture makes the point concrete: state rewards are 0 and 1 and independent of the action, the controlled transition is `[[1/2,1/2],[1/4,3/4]]`, the stationary distribution is `[1/3,2/3]`, the average reward is `2/3` and the stationary derivative is `[-1/18,1/18]` — freezing that distribution gives an immediate-reward gradient of zero, while the true average-reward gradient is `1/18`, so a policy change that only moves where the chain spends its time is invisible to a frozen-distribution calculation. Two separate symmetric chains with state rewards 0/1 have equal one-step stationary reward variance `1/4`, yet staying in the same state with probability `7/8` gives asymptotic reward-sum variance per step `7/4` while deterministic alternation gives zero: the alternating chain has period two, so its exact stationary block-sum variance is zero for even lengths and `1/4` for odd lengths, and the figure shows all lengths 1..16 with both exact limits on the same axis. A centered Poisson solve returns `h` satisfying `(I-P)h=r-rho` and `mu h=0`, and the average-reward gradient is checked in both forms, `d rho = (dmu) r + mu dr = mu dr + mu (dP) h`; for every positive state/action/next-state edge the report returns the centered martingale increment `D=R-rho+h(next)-h(state)` and its nonnegative variance contribution, whose stationary second moment is the asymptotic variance of centered reward sums per step, and exact conditional first/second-moment dynamic programs return every finite-block variance through the requested length without enumerating exponentially many paths. The controlled chain must be irreducible, and periodicity is computed and reported rather than silently ruled out. The discounted extension separates the true fixed-start objective gradient from a stationary proxy: the optional `audit_discounted` API and `discount` CLI compute exact centered resolvents, their parameter derivatives and every residual, distinguishing the stationary average-reward gradient proxy from the true gradient of `(1-beta) E_start[sum beta^t R_t]` with the starting state fixed, and returning discounted occupancy, proxy bias and the antisymmetric derivative matrix — curl, not a full Jacobian. On a separate two-coordinate fixture with controlled switch probabilities `1/2,1/4`, derivatives `1/8,1/16` and average-reward gradient `[1/18,-1/18]`, at beta `1/2` the proxy is `[1/21,-1/21]` while the true fixed-start objective is `2/7` with gradient `[5/98,-1/98]`, and the proxy's exact curl entry is `-1/147`, ruling out a local scalar potential — though zero curl alone is not a sufficient global-potential test. At beta `1` the audit solves the centered Poisson limit instead of inverting the singular discounted matrix, recovering the true average-reward gradient and zero curl, including periodic chains; beta input is at most eight distinct rational pairs through denominator 10,000, and no monotone-bias or sampled-estimator claim is made. 39 tests cover 25 analytic two-state transition settings, 16 exact policy-weight gradient settings, nine multi-state/two-coordinate determinant and finite-difference references, explicit path enumerations through four steps for all three fixtures, the alternating chain through length 32, period-three chains, IID action rewards, zero-asymptotic-variance telescoping edge rewards, feature/reward/state/action transformations, five-state complete-graph tree counts, reducible refusal, strict CLI/schema/file bounds, input/output/RNG isolation and every SVG point/limit geometry; ten of those tests check an exact 16-weight/four-beta proxy/curl grid, independent Cramer/determinant finite differences across three models, all seven starting-state choices and four betas, true fixed-start gradients, near-one/zero/one/periodic/IID cases, transformations, all residuals and strict CLI/isolation, and three new figure tests check every exact table cell and its position, full metadata and tamper rejection. Input is a versioned JSON model with 1..5 states, 1..4 actions and 1..3 shared coordinates, with rational probabilities and rewards bounded, transition rows summing exactly to one, and duplicate keys, booleans, floats and malformed shapes rejected; the independent tree reference checks at most 1,280 parent assignments. Two reproducible SVGs and a six-module v0.2 wheel that reproduces the complete discount demo outside checkout, with both original audit and demo outputs unchanged, dependency-free Python 3.10+. Stationary sensitivities, directed spanning-tree formulas, Poisson equations and martingale decompositions are standard — the repository cites Aldous and Fill Section 9.2, Glynn and Infanger's Poisson-equation framework, and Greensmith, Bartlett and Baxter's average-reward policy-gradient context — but this is not GPOMDP, a learned policy, a sampled gradient estimator, a confidence interval, a mixing-rate or CLT result, arbitrary reducible-chain inference or a large-scale RL claim, and reward-sum variance is not policy-gradient estimator variance: exact arithmetic checks the supplied model's calculations, not its realism.
  - Case study: [ergodlab.md](ergodlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/ergodlab](https://github.com/midya8/ergodlab)

- **ScoreLab** - Exact policy-gradient distributions, not noisy training curves, built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the variance cost of a familiar baseline inspectable, on supplied finite models where every trajectory, mean and covariance can be computed exactly. A one-step Bernoulli action with P(action=1)=4/5 and reward equal to the action has expected reward 4/5 and exact gradient 4/25; the exact state-value baseline 4/5 gives per-trajectory gradient variance 36/625 — nine times the 4/625 of no baseline — while the variance-minimizing scalar baseline 1/5 makes both possible actions produce the same estimate 4/25, so its variance is zero: an explicit counterexample, not a claim that value baselines usually hurt. A separate three-step, two-state model with two shared policy parameters has 64 positive-probability trajectories and exact gradient [1238843/7962624, -4477/31104]; trace covariances are 5.4508 for the full-return score function, 2.2574 for reward-to-go with no baseline, 1.5773 with the exact time/state value baseline and 1.4625 with the variance-minimizing time/state baseline. The optimal scalar baseline at each time/state node is E[Z_j · G0]/E[||Z_j||²], using the whole reward-to-go gradient vector, not a local reward-weighted average or the exact state value, and ScoreLab returns both sides of the exact excess-variance identity for every supplied baseline. Matrix score controls go further: a fixed matrix B Z at each time/state lets every output coordinate use every score coordinate, minimizing the whole covariance matrix in positive-semidefinite order within that class. On a one-step three-action policy with probabilities 1/6, 1/3, 1/2, features (0,0), (1,0), (0,1) and rewards 0, 1, 2, the exact gradient is [-1/9, 1/3]: no baseline leaves trace variance 269/324, the exact state-value baseline 29/324, the best scalar baseline (64/51) 53/612, and the best matrix control — the exact matrix [[2/3,-2/3],[-1/2,1]] — gives zero on every action: a supplied finite-model example, not a general zero-variance method. On the separate 64-path sequential model the gain is much smaller, scalar-optimal trace 1.462519 against matrix-optimal 1.460918, and its time/state score ranks are [1,0,1,1,1,1], so pretending every score covariance has an inverse would fail; rational RREF solves the singular normal equations with free coordinates set to zero, not a pseudoinverse, and every covariance gap is returned with its positive-semidefinite node terms. 36 tests cover 256 one-step Bernoulli policies with analytic gradients and variance formulas, 24 shared-parameter sequential settings with 48 coordinate derivatives checked by a separate dual-number Cartesian path oracle plus central finite differences, all covariance entries against an independent enumeration, 15 matrix-control settings with exact cross moments and principal minors, and two reproducible SVGs — one embedding the complete exact demo as compressed metadata — with byte-regeneration checks; strict JSON CLI, 1..3 states/actions/features, horizon 0..6, 64 KiB input cap and a six-module wheel verified outside the checkout, Python 3.10..3.13, standard library only. The variance-reduction reasoning follows Greensmith, Bartlett and Baxter (JMLR 2004, Sections 4–6), but ScoreLab does not implement GPOMDP or reproduce that paper's experiments — supplied finite models only, with no environment fitting, actor-critic training, continuous state/action method, learned baseline or large-scale policy-gradient performance claim.
  - Case study: [scorelab.md](scorelab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/scorelab](https://github.com/midya8/scorelab)

- **BanditLab** - Exact finite-budget Bayesian bandit planning from scratch in pure Python — it makes the value of learning before committing inspectable, on supplied priors where every posterior, policy value and regret can be computed exactly. Two independent Beta-Bernoulli arms: arm 0 has prior Beta(1,1), mean 1/2; arm 1 has prior Beta(5,4), mean 5/9. Each arm's unknown success rate is drawn once from its prior and then stays fixed; pulling an arm returns a unit Bernoulli reward and updates only that arm's posterior. With one pull, choose arm 1; with two pulls both first arms tie and the adaptive-greedy gain is zero; with three pulls the optimal adaptive policy starts with arm 0 despite its smaller current mean — if that first pull succeeds, choose arm 0 next; if it fails, choose arm 1 next — and the full returned state graph determines the last action too. Over the same three-pull budget the optimal adaptive policy expects 187/108, the fully updating posterior-mean greedy policy 46/27, the best fixed-arm commitment 5/3, and a clairvoyant that knows the latent rates beforehand 2 as an upper bound — the adaptive gain over greedy is exactly 1/36, and greedy is not frozen: it updates its posterior after every outcome and picks a new highest-mean arm at every remaining decision. The six evaluated budgets are 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 pulls; the first SVG's lines connect those values plus the known zero-budget origin. A forward policy audit propagates exact probability mass separately from backward conditional values and accounts for every lost reward as an occupancy-weighted Bellman gap: over three pulls the optimal policy averages 11/6 arm-0 pulls across 14 of 35 graph states with zero loss, greedy averages 2/3 arm-0 pulls across the same 14 states with loss 1/36, and fixed arm 1 averages 0 across 10 of 35 states with loss 7/108 — greedy's entire 1/36 loss is its first-decision gap, while fixed's contributions are 1/36, 8/297 and 1/99, which sum to 7/108, a finite-horizon performance-difference identity, not a causal experiment. An exact information-advantaged upper bound integrates the product of the arms' Beta CDFs: in the demo E[max(theta_0,theta_1)]=2/3, so at three pulls the optimal Bayes regret is 29/108. Strict inputs cap the problem at 1..3 arms, integer starting shapes 1..16, horizon 0..12 and 64 KiB CLI files, with an exact state preflight — binomial(H+2K,2K) posterior/time states — checked before any generation: two arms at horizon 12 need 1,820 states, three arms at horizon 8 need 3,003, and three arms at horizon 12 need 18,564, above the default 4,096 state limit. 34 tests include every complete depth-three two-arm policy tree for 16 prior pairs (2,048 references checked by an independent Beta-moment oracle), every complete depth-two three-arm tree for 64 prior triples (1,728 references, plus a 462-state three-arm graph audit), all 256 initial Beta shape pairs against an independent binomial/Bernstein CDF expression, and 144 independent named-policy history references across 16 prior pairs and three horizons, with two reproducible SVGs, a strict JSON CLI and an isolated six-module wheel. The Bayesian information-state formulation follows Min, Maglaras and Moallemi's Thompson Sampling with Information Relaxation Penalties (Sections 2 and 3), but BanditLab implements the small full finite-state Bellman problem, not that paper's IRS algorithms, Thompson sampling, Gittins indices or a large-scale bandit method. Prescribed supplied-prior calculations only, not simulated trials, empirical bandit rankings, confidence intervals or real-world decision safety.
  - Case study: [banditlab.md](banditlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/banditlab](https://github.com/midya8/banditlab)

- **BeliefLab** - Exact rational finite-horizon POMDP planning for two hidden states, built from scratch in pure Python (3.10+, standard library only) — it makes the value of paying for information inspectable, on supplied models where every belief, tree and envelope can be computed exactly. A directly specified sensing fixture has two hidden states, three actions and a two-action horizon at discount one and uniform initial belief: exploit action 0 rewards +1 in state 0 and -1 in state 1, exploit action 1 reverses those rewards, both with uninformative observations, while Probe costs 1/10 now and reports the state correctly with probability 9/10. A myopic one-action decision picks an exploit action — immediate reward zero beats probe's -1/10 — but two-action planning probes, then exploits on the observation: each observation has probability 1/2, producing posterior (9/10,1/10) or (1/10,9/10), expected second reward 4/5, total -1/10 + 4/5 = 7/10, against zero for either exploit-first sequence over the same horizon. For any starting belief b the exact two-step value is max(2(1-2b), 7/10, 2(2b-1)); Probe is optimal on [13/40,27/40], including a tie at either boundary, and outside that interval the more likely state is exploited. An optimized open-loop baseline checks every fixed action sequence over the same model and horizon — nine two-step sequences, at most 81 under scope — and still uses the correct transition dynamics and every action's reward: at the uniform belief four exploit-only sequences tie at value zero while the contingent optimum is 7/10, the exact feedback gap peaks at 7/10 at b=1/2 with uniform-belief integral 49/400, and a declared control that changes every observation row to (1/2,1/2) and nothing else has gap exactly zero at every belief. A forward execution audit traces every observation history at every time, including impossible histories without fabricated posteriors: state mass is the unconditional joint probability of history and current state, its sum is the reach probability, and summing discounted reward mass over all decisions gives the policy value — the probe policy's uniform-prior accounting is -1/10 + (1/2)(4/5) + (1/2)(4/5) = 7/10, each branch contributing 2/5, not 4/5, and a separate backward tree evaluation must agree exactly with the forward sum. Fixed-tree regret certificates compare a supplied tree against the optimized envelope over every exact interval: the probe tree is optimal on [13/40,27/40] but loses 13/10 at either certain-state prior, and an exploit-0-twice tree has uniform-prior value zero, regret 7/10 and worst regret 4 at belief0=0 while being optimal on [27/40,1] — being optimal at the uniform prior does not make one fixed tree optimal at all priors. Discounted all-belief Bellman-residual brackets connect finite envelopes to infinite optimal value without pretending the finite tree runs forever: a prescribed constant reward 1/100 at discount 99/100 has horizon-zero residual 1/100 but infinite value 1, so the raw residual is not a value-error certificate, and a negative-reward control gives residual -1/100 and exact infinite value -1; a separate perfect-probe fixture (probe perfectly revealing at cost 1/10, discount 1/2) has analytic infinite value max(2|2b-1|, 9/10), and at uniform belief its horizon-three value is 13/20 with global residual range [31/600,1/8], signed shifts [31/300,1/4] and certified infinite-value interval [113/150,9/10] against true value 9/10. 86 tests cover 81 model settings against 2,187 complete depth-two policy trees, a separate 2,187-tree depth-three check, 729 fixed sequences, exact crossing cells, full joint path occupancies and independent belief recursion, with four reproducible SVGs, strict JSON CLI, preflight caps and an isolated ten-module wheel. Supplied-model calculations only, not learned information value, empirical calibration, causal sensor performance or real-world safety.
  - Case study: [belieflab.md](belieflab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/belieflab](https://github.com/midya8/belieflab)

- **BellmanLab** - Inspect exact values in small supplied discounted models. A residual control separates a small Bellman residual from a small value error. Separate rectangular-scenario and nested-policy workflows expose scenario-row choices, evaluated policies and cap failures. One- and two-budget browser inspectors show occupancy flow, visit-reweighted randomized policies and independently checked primal-dual or infeasibility certificates. Python uses exact fractions; the browser uses BigInt rationals. These are supplied finite dynamics and expected costs, not learned models, per-trajectory safety guarantees or a scalable optimizer. The sources are named, not gestured at: the rectangular robust-DP formulation follows Iyengar, Robust Dynamic Programming (2005), at https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/moor.1040.0129, and Nilim and El Ghaoui, Robust Control of Markov Decision Processes with Uncertain Transition Matrices (2005), at https://people.eecs.berkeley.edu/~elghaoui/Pubs/RobMDP_OR2005.pdf, and the dynamic programming and policy improvement follow Sutton and Barto, Chapter 4. The finite-scenario contract, exact certificates, fixtures, tests and figures are original; the formulations are theirs, and none of this is a new algorithm.
BellmanLab 0.4 adds one supplied expected-cost budget to the discounted MDP, and the budget can make every high-reward deterministic policy infeasible while a randomized stationary policy stays optimal. The live inspector shows the whole cost/reward polygon with its clipped feasible vertices, the exact occupancy flow, the state-conditional policy and an independently checked primal-dual certificate. The wait/exit control makes the distinction exact: mixing two deterministic policies half-and-half gives a local exit probability of 1/3, not 1/2, because the mixture re-rolls the policy at every visit while the randomized action re-rolls only the action. A separate one-state control attains reward 1 under a budget of 1/2 while the best feasible deterministic policy earns 0, and when no policy can meet the budget the planner returns a checked minimum-cost lower bound instead of a policy that fits. 88 Python tests and four Node tests retain all 70 earlier tests: a separate checker rebuilds every report from determinant/Cramer solves and gift wrapping rather than the generator's helpers, the browser runs a separately implemented BigInt engine, and across 861 exhaustive, seeded and control inputs the complete browser reports equal native output while 1,722 actual SciPy HiGHS LP solves agree within 1e-8; an independent full-flow vertex oracle covers 28 cases and 169 non-input primitive-leaf forgeries are rejected. BellmanLab 0.5 keeps both expected costs visible and enumerates the full feasible occupancy vertices, not only their two-dimensional projection: one original three-action control needs a mixture of three deterministic policies, with weights 5/12, 1/3 and 1/4, to attain reward 3/2 under ceilings 1/3 and 1/4, while the best feasible deterministic policy earns zero, and a different control has ceilings that are separately attainable but jointly impossible, with a checked gap of 1/6. Visit rates reweight an initial policy mixture into local action probabilities, so a feasible reward/cost projection alone is not a full policy witness. 103 Python tests and eight Node tests retain the original and one-budget workflows: an independent BigInt browser engine agrees on 822 complete reports, including a dense four-state case beyond JavaScript's safe integer range, and actual SciPy 1.15.3 HiGHS checks all 822 problems — 692 feasible and 130 infeasible — with worst reward error below 4.5e-10; 958 rejected non-input primitive-leaf mutations and isolated installed-wheel checks of both new CLI entry points. Both layers stay inside small supplied models — at most four states and eight total actions, one budget in 0.4 and exactly two ceilings in 0.5 — and budgets are expectations under the supplied initial distribution, not trajectory caps; neither layer is a new algorithm, a learned model, a scale benchmark or a per-trajectory safety guarantee.
  - Case study: [bellmanlab.md](bellmanlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/bellmanlab](https://github.com/midya8/bellmanlab)

- **VariLab** - Inference for supplied Ising models, with a full finite-state reference, mean-field coordinate histories, rational Hessian sign certificates, evidence-aware forest sum-product and compatible joint MAP/count/traceback. The two-spin counterexample has a higher ELBO and lower reverse KL but worse marginal means; a zero-residual coordinate fixed point is not an ELBO maximum. Forest methods allow 128 spins. Bounded cyclic sum-product and rational joint MAP allow 64 spins and 8 query indices, with symbolic work preflight before numeric tables. Independent marginal modes or tied local choices need not form a joint optimum. Current checks: 183 tests, six reproducible SVGs and an installed wheel. Enumeration, mean field and curvature are capped at 12 spins; floating enumeration is not rational probability arithmetic. Rational scores and curvature concern represented binary-float inputs. Resource counts are not time or RAM, refused orders are not impossible models, and no learning, causal, calibrated-uncertainty or optimizer-convergence claim follows.
  - Case study: [varilab.md](varilab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/varilab](https://github.com/midya8/varilab)

- **RobustLab** - Exact linear threat-set audits built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the gap between clean accuracy and certified robustness measurable instead of asserted, for the small class where the inner attack problem can actually be solved: binary affine scores under L1, L2 and L-infinity balls. For signed margin y*(w.x+b), the worst margin in an Lp ball is the clean margin minus epsilon times the dual weight norm, and the implementation returns the actual perturbation attaining that margin — a signed coordinate vertex for L1, an opposite weight direction for L2, a signed corner for L-infinity — plus the attacked coordinates, attained signed margin and zero-margin radius. Both training arms start from zero on the same 30 fixed synthetic rows for 4,000 updates at rate 0.1 with L2 coefficient 0.001, and the only changed training setting is the L-infinity radius, 0 versus 0.30, with no test-driven stopping, seed search or checkpoint selection: ordinary logistic reaches 100% clean accuracy on the shifted holdout but 20% strict robust accuracy at radius 0.30 and fails the >=75% robust gate, while robust logistic accepts 80% clean for 80% robust and passes — a constructed descriptive comparison on a supplied factorial grid, not an image benchmark or a universal claim about the cost of robustness. The robust objective puts the worst margin inside softplus with a separate explicit quadratic weight regularizer; hand-derived selected subgradients are checked against central finite differences away from kinks and against convex subgradient inequalities at zero/tied weights, and the optimizer is fixed-step full-batch descent, not an asserted exact minimizer. A separate exact-audit API makes the float limitation actionable for L1 and L-infinity balls: it interprets each validated input as its actual stored binary float, returns scores, dual norms, radii, worst/attained margins, perturbations and attacked coordinates as reduced numerator/denominator strings, and sets certificate flags from integer signs rather than rounded decimal views — in a selected boundary fixture the float worst margin is approximately +2.081668171e-17 while the rational worst margin is approximately -2.775557562e-18, so the float strict flag is true while the exact flag is false, with a reverse-disagreement fixture and a dyadic exact-zero control that fails both strict flags; these are selected edge cases from a documented finite decimal grid search, not prevalence measurements, and L2 is rejected explicitly because general directions and margins can be irrational. The box-audit API solves L1/L-infinity balls intersected with supplied per-coordinate bounds and returns exact rational primal movement plus a matching dual gain bound — L1 is bounded fractional knapsack, L-infinity separates by coordinate — and a direct dyadic fixture makes the failure mode concrete: weights (4,1), bias -1/4, point (1/16,3/4), positive label and unit box, at L1 radius 5/8 clipping the old all-real attack leaves margin +1/2 while the actual minimum is -1/16, attained by movements (-1/16,-9/16), with exact primal and dual gains both equal to 13/16 and zero gap, so the positive clipped candidate cannot prove robustness; under L-infinity, independent adverse-direction clipping is optimal. 80 unit tests, including independent rational corner enumeration over 240 small model/label configurations, an L2 3-4-5 identity and 10,000-direction circle check, exact first optimizer update and final objective, a separate full orthant-polytope vertex oracle on 468 box configurations, strict CLI errors and resource preflight at a 4,096 row-coordinate-product cap, three reproducible audit SVGs with all plotted values retained in numeric metadata, and CI on Python 3.10..3.13, standard library only. The original training and all-real audit threat domain has no clipping, boxes or preprocessing; the bias is fixed; strict robustness requires worst signed margin >0 and a boundary tie fails conservatively; the identity is exact in real arithmetic but evaluated in ordinary floats, not interval or outward-rounded arithmetic — no nonlinear or multiclass certificate, adaptive attack suite, distributional generalization, calibrated uncertainty or production security guarantee is claimed.
  - Case study: [robustlab.md](robustlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/robustlab](https://github.com/midya8/robustlab)

- **CausalLab** - Exact finite structural causal models built from scratch in pure Python — it keeps observation, intervention and counterfactual queries separate, so a strong association cannot be mistaken for what replacing a mechanism would do. A supplied 100-world confounding fixture reverses a +.26 observed association into a -.10 supplied-model intervention difference, and backdoor adjustment using the supplied Z distribution matches intervention enumeration — a synthetic counterexample, not an empirical treatment effect or policy recommendation.
