Two supplied mechanisms can share the identical observed joint yet disagree on actions and counterfactuals: with X=U, Y=X versus Y=U, P(Y=1 given do(X=1)) is 1 versus .5, and given factual X=0,Y=0 the probability Y would be 1 under X=1 is 1 versus 0. Named multi-world counterfactual joints expose shared-noise response dependence that identical action marginals hide: stable (Y=U) and flip (Y=X xor U) models both have a uniform observed joint and P(Y=1 | do(X=x))=.5 under either action, yet their (Y0,Y1) joints are (.50, 0, 0, .50) versus (0, .50, .50, 0) while the naive product puts .25 everywhere — factual X=0,Y=0 gives (0,0) for stable and (0,1) for flip. Sharp binary response bounds return exact rational attaining joint witnesses from point action marginals instead of choosing a coupling: with .50/.50 marginals the helped and harmed fractions each range [0,.50] with mean effect .00, and with .25/.75 the helped fraction ranges [.50,.75], harmed [0,.25], mean effect +.50; an independent rational reference enumerates all 165 denominator-eight response joints across 81 marginal pairs and checks exact extrema with and without monotonicity, and optional explicit monotonicity rules out flip while zero-probability conditioning rates stay explicitly undefined. The graph-only d-separation API uses ancestor restriction, moralization and connectivity with certificates — ancestor/moral-edge certificates, collider-descendant checks and explicit incoming-edge surgery — and agrees with an independent reference on 49,152 singleton/conditioning/surgery combinations and 7,040 disjoint set queries across all 64 four-node DAGs consistent with one fixed order. A bounded backdoor-set search checks every subset of 0..10 explicitly available candidates and exposes collider bias: in the fixed A→X, A→C←B, B→Y, X→Y graph the empty set passes while adding C alone opens a collider and fails, so search does not prune by assuming valid supersets; an independent reference agrees on 3,072 adjustment sets across 768 searches, and a positive-support adjustment calculation of .60/.50 matches direct interventions. The frontdoor stage checks the established sufficient frontdoor criterion with all three conditions visible even when several fail — directed bypass witnesses and two full ancestral-moral certificates — and an exact-rational functional from a supplied positive joint; the credited latent-confounding motif and a fixed synthetic XOR fixture separate observed conditionals 41/112 and 37/80 from the frontdoor functional 7/16 and 9/16. 164 tests, independent rational references including all 64 tiny Boolean mechanism pairs, six reproducible audit SVGs and CI on Python 3.10..3.13, standard library only. Supplied mechanisms are assumptions, not causes learned from observational data — exhaustive finite enumeration in floating point, with no graph discovery, automatic do-calculus identification, continuous or cyclic models, or empirical confidence intervals.
  - Case study: [causallab.md](causallab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/causallab](https://github.com/midya8/causallab)

- **ParticleLab** - A scalar bootstrap particle filter built from scratch in pure Python — it audits what a tidy weight summary hides: equal weights and full effective sample size can still mean every particle descends from one badly placed initial draw. Log-domain likelihood weighting, adaptive systematic resampling, filtered versus carried summaries, and full particles/weights/log weights/parent mappings/root-label histories, against an exact scalar linear-Gaussian Kalman reference with an explicit no-transition-at-t0 convention. On a fixed twelve-point linear example with 512 particles over all sixteen seeds 0..15, the mean of per-seed mean RMSEs versus the exact Kalman filter is .0161016904 and the variance RMSE average is .00794734279, with seed 0 plotted without selecting for fit — model-conditional approximation errors, not real-world accuracy or calibration. The support-collapse fixture (twenty zeros, a=1, process std 0, observation std .1, initial mean 0/std 3, 32 particles, seed 0) makes the failure concrete: with resampling threshold fraction 1 the run ends at weight ESS 32 with 1 initial label carried and latent variance 0, while resampling disabled ends at ESS 1.00002950 with 32 labels carried and latent variance .00000308085, against an exact reference mean 0 and latent variance .000499972224 — full ESS after resampling does not recover missing support, and with zero process noise neither method can create a missing state value. A restricted static square-observation reference has exact mean 0 and sign probability 1/2 with modes near +/-2, yet a selected eight-particle run loses one sign while carried ESS is 8/8. A separate terminal-weighted genealogy smoother traces retained parent maps without drawing new paths: on a fixed 64-step dynamic fixture ending with 32 distinct current states but only one retained initial path, time-0 particle variance is 0 while exact smoothed variance is .009521314, and across all sixteen inherited linear seeds the smoothing mean/variance RMSEs versus the exact scalar RTS reference are .0299202464/.0137440904. Optional backward simulation connects retained filtering states outside the parent maps: 512 draws reach two initial indices instead of one genealogical path, with time-0 variance .022290238 still over twice the exact .009521314, and process std below 1e-4 is rejected rather than fabricating a deterministic-transition density. An optional linear observation-conditioned Gaussian proposal exposes per-particle proposal moments and predictive importance weights: across all sixteen fixed seeds at N=32, mean RMSE is similar (.082871/.081548 bootstrap/conditional) but conditional variance RMSE is worse (.037895 versus .035944) — a more informative observation fixture has smaller conditional errors, not a general or causal ranking. 114 unit tests, strict JSON CLI, five reproducible SVGs — the particle-audit figure (filtering, variance, ESS and ancestry) plus backward, proposal, smoothing and square-symmetry audits — all regenerated byte-identical, with the particle-audit figure retaining all plotted values in numeric SVG metadata, and CI on Python 3.10..3.13, standard library only. The documentation review is merged in PR #1, main 06832dbab58fa99e1e0573e904ea645ab07b8e20: fresh current, revised, published-head and merged-main checkouts each passed 114 Python tests and the five SVG regeneration checks, installed-wheel checks verified seven module origins and ten complete CLI reports outside the checkout, all 43 published and main files matched the reviewed checkout with 41 tracked files unchanged, and head, PR and merged-main CI each passed all five jobs. A scalar supplied-parameter model with finite particles and floating point — no convergence certificate, high-dimensional proposal, learned parameters, robustness, anomaly p-values or coverage guarantee, and evidence estimates are random with logs that are not unbiased log-evidence estimates.
  - Case study: [particlelab.md](particlelab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/particlelab](https://github.com/midya8/particlelab)

- **SegmentLab** - Exact offline Bayesian Gaussian changepoint inference built from scratch in pure Python — it retains the full finite-partition evidence and marginals, not only one best cut list, so a confident segmentation can be audited instead of trusted. The distinctions it makes inspectable: a single outlier can look like a short, high-confidence segment when the assumed noise is too small; thresholded marginal peaks hide whether cuts occur together; and the most probable whole partition need not minimize expected boundary mistakes. Conjugate Gaussian interval evidence uses centered rolling mean/M2 statistics with no repeated interval rescans; count-capped forward/backward log sums, explicitly normalized admissible Bernoulli-gap priors, all interval/boundary/count marginals and a complete MAP backtrace; pointwise latent-mean uncertainty includes within-segment and between-partition components and excludes future observation noise; seeded complete-partition backward draws plus conditional Gaussian segment means come with finite-RNG limits stated; exact requested-pair cut-inclusion queries allow other cuts, return finite log probabilities, and flag impossible events separately from undefined conditionals; full discrete partition entropy, conditioned-prior KL and dependence of the entire cut vector replace an independent-cut entropy shortcut; constrained minimum expected false/missed boundary-loss decisions keep MAP and infeasible-threshold comparisons separate. On a selected seven-point decision fixture the no-cut decision has lower expected boundary risk, .680000591, than the MAP partition [3] at .869071786 — MAP maximizes posterior probability while `decide` minimizes expected loss under supplied relative costs, and neither number is observed accuracy. The same isolated outlier under two supplied noise scales gives MAP [12,13] at probability .960218 for sigma .15 and MAP [] at .648223 for sigma 2 — model confidence can isolate contamination, not a noise-model quality ranking. On six zeros with a count cap of 2, cuts 2 and 4 each carry marginal mass .011658220257 while their exact joint mass is 0, so multiplying marginals invents a structurally forbidden event; conditioning on an impossible event returns null, not 0. A four-point fixture has posterior entropy 1.029013408 above the prior .836308115 with KL .166962288, so KL is not prior entropy minus posterior entropy. In 10,000 joint draws at seed 19, the four-segment partition K4 has exact mass .000115427 and zero sampled visits — zero visits do not imply zero posterior mass. Same-design replicate mixtures integrate partition and segment-mean uncertainty, then add fresh noise: in a selected five-point outlier fixture a moment-matched Gaussian overstates density at 2 by 1.54 nats, a shape comparison using all fitted observations, not forecasting, leave-one-out evaluation, anomaly p-values or calibrated coverage. And the predictive question takes its own coordinates: `python -m segmentlab predictive examples/sequence.json --queries '[[2,0.0],[3,1.0]]'` asks for the replicate density at value 0.0 for observation 2 and at value 1.0 for observation 3 — [index, value] pairs, not cut positions. Inference is offline — future observations inform earlier cuts — full marginals cost O(N²·K²) work, bounded to 120 observations and 12 requested segments; results are finite sums in floating point, not symbolic arithmetic or formal numerical certificates, and tiny masses can underflow. 104 unit tests, independent exhaustive tiny references, strict JSON infer/sample/joint/information/decide/predictive commands and two regenerable SVGs with all plotted values retained and CI byte-checks. CI runs Python 3.10–13. Development fixtures only — no online alert, forecast, credible-band coverage, robust-outlier model, event attribution, causality or model-misspecification guarantee, and relative decision costs are supplied loss units, not money.
  - Case study: [segmentlab.md](segmentlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/segmentlab](https://github.com/midya8/segmentlab)

- **HamiltonLab** - Hamiltonian Monte Carlo built from scratch in pure Python — an independent implementation of Neal's Hamiltonian dynamics, no new method: leapfrog integration with explicit signed energy errors, a reversibility check via the unflipped momentum, log-domain Metropolis decisions and a complete transition history in which rejected transitions repeat the old state, because dropping the repeats would bias the output. Demo on disclosed analytic targets, four chains per arm, seeds 71–74, 1,000 retained plus 200 discarded transitions each: on a correlated Gaussian with covariance [[1,.95],[.95,1]], HMC at epsilon .15 and L 10 reaches retained acceptance .979–.987 with pooled means .008864/.018598, variances 1.029658/1.016501 and covariance .973860 against analytic 0/0, 1/1 and .95, while a random walk at proposal SD .15 accepts .765–.800 with means .172653/.195747, variances 1.471826/1.499563 and covariance 1.436712 — same transition counts, different work (52,804 potential-plus-gradient callbacks against 4,804), so the comparison is descriptive, not a sampler ranking. The separated mixture — means (−6,0)/(+6,0), identity component covariance, analytic covariance diag(37,1) — is the point of the tool: HMC accepts .997–1.000 yet each chain makes zero x-sign/mode switches, classical unsplit x R-hat is 6.944902, and pooled summaries that look healthy (positive-x share exactly .5, mean x ≈ −.001131, variance x ≈ 36.420599) conceal disconnected chains, because pooled occupancy was induced by the starts, not learned by mixing. `rank_split_rhat` adds split rank-normalized and folded checks: correlated-Gaussian HMC 1.007774/1.001669, random walk 1.742260/1.331213, separated mixture 1.733936/1.021593 — the mixture maximum is smaller than its classical 6.944902, a different statistic, not evidence of better mixing. Two deterministic counterexamples show why the classical value alone is not enough: a scale difference where classical 0.994987 and rank split 0.989949 look low but folded rank split is 2.117248, and a half-chain shift where classical 0.994987 misses the within-chain shift that rank split 2.115500 exposes. A second failure needs no separated modes: at epsilon √2 on the standard 2-D Gaussian, four leapfrog steps are an identity map in exact arithmetic, so four-step chains accept 1.000 with largest coordinate span 2.81e-14 and mean squared accepted jumps 1.29e-30–1.80e-30, against one-step acceptance .570–.710, span 6.231696 and jumps 2.231222–4.027284 — linear stability and unit determinant do not imply exploration. State-independent uniform trajectory lengths 1–4 restore motion on that resonance (acceptance .845–.910, spans 4.601358–6.454790) but still make zero mode switches in the mixture with lengths 1–10, so randomization is not a solution to separated modes. Analytic harmonic-flow references at exact durations show the same lesson from the other side: at T=2π the expected squared jump is 5.999039e-32, and at T=π it is 4 with conditional variance 1.499760e-32 — position only flips sign — so large squared jumps, zero integration error and invariance do not prove exploration. Finite-window observable audits make the observable-dependence explicit: at the sign flip with N=20 the variance of the mean position is 0 while the variance of the mean q² is 2 (20× the IID value), and random-duration mixtures can give zero position correlation with q²-mean variance still 20× IID — zero correlation of one observable is not independence. A deterministic renderer reproduces the observable-window figure (five panels, shared linear axes, N 1–40) from the analytic references, and CI regenerates and byte-checks the SVG. 82 unit tests cover analytic gradients, leapfrog reversibility, energy error, resonance, rank diagnostics, harmonic references, finite-window and mixture audits and CLI. Offline sample/demo/quadratic/diagnose/harmonic/window/window-mixture CLI and reproducible SVGs. Development fixture only — no convergence certificate, ESS/MCSE estimate, mixing-time guarantee or general sampler ranking claim.
  - Case study: [hamiltonlab.md](hamiltonlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/hamiltonlab](https://github.com/midya8/hamiltonlab)

- **TransportLab** - Small entropic transport problems with full plans, explicit marginal errors and separate rounded primal/dual audits. The editable browser differentiates raw-weight normalization and every fixed Sinkhorn update, retaining all plans, tangents, potentials and history rows. One early-weight control gives full tangent 0.010665526 versus requested-marginal candidate -0.019162331; actual marginal-tangent residual terms explain the difference. Twelve controls include null normalized directions, rectangular inputs, zero support and underflow. 62 Python and six Node/actual-Chrome tests, independent Decimal80 differences, native/browser full reports and installed replay. The earlier 46 tests and outputs remain. No adaptive-stop or rounding derivative, changing-support sensitivity, implicit optimum or certified-bound claim.
  - Case study: [transportlab.md](transportlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/transportlab](https://github.com/midya8/transportlab)

- **FlowLab** - Affine-coupling normalizing flow from scratch in pure Python — exact 2-D model log density, explicit inverse sampling, bounded log scales and handwritten likelihood gradients, not an exact fit: four alternating affine-coupling layers map data x to standard-normal latent z with a shared 1 -> tanh(12) -> 2 conditioner, scale bounded as tanh(raw_scale) while translations and data are not clipped, and a 200-parameter demo trained by manual backpropagation and minibatch Adam. Demo on a disclosed banana-shaped fixture, x=u, y=0.5*v+0.6*(u*u-1) with independent u,v ~ N(0,1) — 1,024 training points (seed 53), 512 fresh test points (seed 59), flow seed 11, training-draw seed 12, 2,000 updates, batch 32, learning rate 0.003, final checkpoint with no validation or test-based selection and no architecture, schedule or seed sweep: the trained flow gives test NLL 2.188408 against 2.907770 for a train-fitted full-covariance Gaussian and 2.896854 for the same flow untrained, with the known generating density 2.151426 as a report-only reference; train NLLs are 2.146052, 2.811894 and 2.808904 against 2.136111 for the generator in the same order — descriptive fixture comparisons, not a causal decomposition or a general model ranking. Test roundtrip/logdet residual max 1.78e-15 is an implementation audit, not a global error bound. Sample residuals are reported rather than smoothed over: on 256-sample arms (sample seed 19, fresh generator reference seed 61) the trained and generated curve-residual second moments are 0.293054 and 0.244743 against 1.720324 for the untrained flow and 1.714797 for the Gaussian, with fractions inside |residual| ≤ 1 of 91.41% and 93.36% against 55.86% and 55.86% in the same order, and a known population residual second moment of 0.25 — sample summaries, not calibrated probability coverage. The gradient CLI and Flow.input_score return the fitted log density, the full input score, the normal-base pullback and the log-Jacobian gradient: with the trained weights fixed on a 9×9 grid (x=-2..2, y=-1..3, spacing .5), central differences at epsilon 1e-5 give max coordinate error 1.1904095487125232e-9, while leaving the volume term out raises the max coordinate discrepancy to 0.6605454933292365 — the base term alone is not the score, and a dropped term still returns a plausible-looking vector of the right shape. Tests check both coordinates and both components across 1/2/3/8 layers plus independent standard-normal, constant-affine and variable-scale formulas; the CLI takes 1–256 rows and rejects nonfinite vectors. Conditional slices evaluate the joint log density along one free coordinate with log-max subtraction against underflow, and the trapezoid rule normalizes a piecewise-linear density on the supplied finite interval — moments integrate the interpolation analytically, CDF integrates each segment, quantiles invert by bounded bisection, and the JSON retains every grid point, the original joint log density, normalized density, CDF, requested quantiles, moments and actual model-call count, with both fixed-axis choices working and no unreported adaptive search. The window audit reuses one outer grid and a contiguous, index-aligned subgrid for the inner window with no second round of model evaluations: outer [-4,4] at 513 points, inner indices 192..320 giving [-1,1] at 129 points, 1,539 model evaluations total, and narrowing the window removes 24.35%–31.94% of interpolated mass within the finite outer window (0.243537 at fixed x=0, 0.297166 at x=-1.5, 0.319448 at x=1.5) with inner-minus-outer median shifts +0.161078, -0.191406 and -0.226502, inner medians -0.471317 at x=0, 0.534865 at x=-1.5 and 0.495517 at x=1.5, and inner variances 0.144531, 0.119903 and 0.127310 in the same order — the removed fractions are not whole-line tail probabilities, they exclude everything outside [-4,4] and may miss structure between grid points, and the inner curve is a new renormalized distribution, not a crop of the outer one. A non-neural adversarial integrand, .0001 + .5*NormalPDF(y; mean=.015625, sd=.0001) on [0,1], shows why grid agreement cannot certify conditional accuracy: both 17- and 33-point grids miss the same narrow peak and report integral 0.0001 against the analytic normal-CDF reference 0.5001, with interpolated means 0.5 despite the actual mean being near 0.01572 — a demonstration about grids, not evidence that the trained model has the same missed peak. 45 tests cover every parameter of tiny flows by central finite differences, an independent finite-difference full Jacobian determinant, both inverse directions, constant-affine density, logdet sign, standard-normal identity, midpoint density integration, strict serialization, bounds, no mutation and CLI, plus aligned window reuse/counts, constant-density offset invariance, exact full-window identity, nested mass monotonicity, the normal erf reference and the missed-peak counterexample; CI runs Python 3.10–3.13, trains the actual fixture and checks all four reproduced SVGs on 3.11. Strict artifacts, offline train/score/gradient/sample/conditional/windows CLI and reproducible SVGs. An independent educational implementation of Dinh, Sohl-Dickstein and Bengio (2017), Density estimation using Real NVP (arXiv:1605.08803v3) — change of variables Eq. 2–3, affine coupling and inverse Eq. 4–9, alternating couplings Section 3.5 — source read for the mathematics, no copied code or pretrained weights, and the paper's image architecture and benchmarks are not reproduced. Development fixture only — no exact-fit, image, anomaly-detection, calibrated-coverage, full-line-normalization or general ranking claim.
  - Case study: [flowlab.md](flowlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/flowlab](https://github.com/midya8/flowlab)

- **GraphLab** - Two-layer graph convolutional network built from scratch in pure Python — an independent educational implementation of Kipf and Welling (2017), standard GCN theory, no new method: symmetric normalized propagation S = D^(-1/2)(A + I)D^(-1/2) with H = ReLU(S X W0) and P = softmax(S H W1), applied through sparse neighbor lists — not row normalization — with self loops keeping isolated nodes' own features, and a feature-only MLP control using identity propagation with the same parameterization, initialization, parameter count and update budget. Cross-entropy gradients use training nodes only; validation labels select a checkpoint, including initialization, but never enter the training gradient, and test labels are passed as None during fitting — test features and edges stay visible, so this is transductive node evaluation on one graph, not unseen-graph generalization. Demo on a seed-41 fixture (96 nodes in two equal class communities, 4 noisy features, 483 edges; class means ±0.25, noise SD 1; edges sampled with probability 0.2 within a class and 0.015 across; 8 training + 8 validation + 32 test nodes per class; graph construction deliberately carries class signal): each arm has 72 weights, shared seed 13, Adam learning rate 0.03 and 300 updates, with validation cross-entropy selecting each arm independently among initialization and every update, earliest ties retained — the selected GCN reaches test accuracy 0.96875 (test CE 0.060893) against 0.34375 for the feature-only MLP (selected update 1, CE 0.848734) and 0.515625 for a scrambled-graph GCN (seed 47 relabel, selected update 0, CE 0.693328), with a train-only nearest-class-centroid control at 0.484375 and balanced constant predictions at 0.5 — descriptive fixture results, not causal contributions or a general GCN ranking. A six-cycle and two disconnected triangles with identical constant features and degree 2 give identical node probabilities, an exact counterexample for this architecture, not a claim about all graph networks. `margin_attribution` / `explain` differentiates a target node's chosen class-logit margin with respect to all supplied input coordinates, returning full signed gradient and gradient-times-input matrices, per-node sums, the margin, the sum residual and the two-hop receptive field: on a hand-set path fixture the node contributions are [0.181666667, 0.341907943, 0.074981603, 0, 0, 0], summing to margin 0.5985562126 with residual 0.0, and node 2's first coordinate contributes −0.0136082763, so unsigned magnitudes alone would change the answer — for this bias-free, positively homogeneous ReLU model and the all-zero reference the signed x·gradient sum equals the margin, but a one-node ReLU(x) counterexample at x=−1 has zero gradient and zero x·gradient while replacing x with +1 moves the margin from 0 to 1, so local derivatives do not measure arbitrary finite interventions. `edge_sensitivity` / `edges` is a different question: it reruns the fixed model after deleting each single original edge separately and recomputing all degrees and normalization, returning every signed margin change — node 3's input-feature derivative is zero, yet deleting edge 2-3 changes the target margin by +0.016851731 because node 2's degree changes; the nearer deletions move it more, edge 0-1 by +0.143443787 and edge 1-2 by +0.121943787, while deletions 3-4 and 4-5 have no effect in this fixture. A preflight budget requires 1 + number of edges model forwards before any model call (default cap 256, supported 1–8193; the five-edge demo takes exactly six), and an over-budget request fails rather than silently sampling. Small undirected unweighted graphs only (1–128 nodes, 1–16 features, hidden width 1–32, 2–8 classes), no edge features, attention, pooling, minibatching, dropout, weight decay, gradient clipping, calibration or confidence guarantee; the paper's full training recipe and citation-network benchmarks are not reproduced. 42 unit tests cover every parameter of tiny reference models by finite differences in both modes, sparse-vs-dense forward arithmetic, independent degree normalization, permutation-equivariant predictions and training, mask isolation, stable unclipped cross-entropy, selection vs independent prefix refits, artifacts and CLI. Strict artifacts, offline train/predict/explain/edges CLI and reproducible SVGs. Development fixture only — no causal, probability-attribution, unseen-graph or general ranking claim. Joint deletion is now a complete census rather than an additive guess: 51 Python tests retain all 42 originals, with 180 seeded dense/bitmask cases, 70-digit Decimal checks of all 16 counterexample sets, Python/JavaScript agreement on 46 reports and 559 subsets, and NumPy checking all 559 logit vectors (max difference 8.88e-16). In the hand-set counterexample (features [-4,-4,6,6], five edges, baseline margin 0.5534180126) all five single deletions preserve class 1, yet the pair e1=(0,2), e3=(1,2) gives margin -0.6666666667 and class 0 against a singleton extrapolation of +0.3151513784 (residual -0.9818180451); five of the 16 empty/single/pair sets change the class, and the minimum-margin pair e1/e2 reaches -1.0606601718. Preflight sums binomial coefficients through min(k,m) (native limits k=0..8, 1..65,536 forwards); the 483-edge graph at k=2 exceeds the cap while k=1 fits. Five Node/Chrome tests cover 24 responsive views, 568 subset selections and six natively replayed trusted downloads. Bounded binary64 fixed-model sensitivity census over original edges only — not a causal effect or a new algorithm. [Source](https://github.com/midya8/graphlab). [Design contracts](https://github.com/midya8/graphlab/blob/main/DESIGN.md).
  - Case study: [graphlab.md](graphlab.md)

- **DiffuseLab** - Learned 2-D diffusion from scratch in pure Python — standard DDPM theory, no new method: a linear beta schedule, closed-form forward noising and a 1-based timestep contract, with a `7 -> tanh(48) -> 2` noise predictor (two coordinates, normalized time and four sine/cosine features; 482 learned parameters, exact MSE gradients, minibatch Adam) and epsilon-parameterized DDPM reverse means using the forward posterior variance as fixed reverse variance — no final-step noise, clipping, EMA, learned variance or checkpoint selection. Demo on a development fixture of eight equally likely centers on a radius-2 circle with isotropic noise SD 0.12 (1,024 training points, seed 31; 256 fresh reference points, seed 37; network seed 7, training draws seed 8, sample seed 17; no seed, architecture, schedule or parameter search): with 256 samples the learned DDPM reaches all 8/8 modes with mean nearest-center distance 0.378199, 60.55% within 0.4 and symmetric nearest-neighbor squared distance 0.037103, against 20.100807/0.00%/0/8/259.754704 for the same network untrained and 0.977930/7.42%/1/8/0.391052 for a standard Gaussian prior — the untrained chain is an implementation control, not a competitive baseline. Fresh-draw noise-prediction MSE (seed 43) is 0.356809 trained vs 0.962031 untrained. The learned model is imperfect: 39.45% of samples sit farther than 0.4 from any center and nearest-center bins are [16, 36, 23, 28, 23, 37, 43, 50], not balanced; a deliberately collapsed generator passes center-distance metrics but fails coverage and the symmetric metric in a test. The same trained model samples by DDIM on a selected timestep grid with eta in [0,1] — no retraining or checkpoint change: 8 steps saves 87.5% of predictor calls (61.72% within 0.4, 8/8 modes) and 16 steps saves 75% (61.72%), against DDPM's 64 calls at 60.55% — but the larger near-center fraction does not establish better quality, since every DDIM arm has worse symmetric reference distance (0.210661 at 8 steps, 0.188649 at 16, 0.175727 at 64, vs DDPM 0.037103); full-grid eta=1 matches DDPM arithmetic with matched draws, and call savings count actual predictor invocations, not measured runtime, FLOPs or memory. Strict diffuselab_v1 JSON artifacts, seeded training and sampling, offline train/sample/DDIM CLI, 33 unit tests and Python 3.10–3.13 CI. Development fixture only — no walltime speedup, image-generation or general ranking claim, and the fixture was visible during development, not an untouched benchmark.
  - Case study: [diffuselab.md](diffuselab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/diffuselab](https://github.com/midya8/diffuselab)
- **ChainLab** - Separate prefix evidence, supported path decisions and whole-path uncertainty in hidden Markov models. The rational workbench shows MAP, supported expected matches and supported product marginals choosing different paths, while unconstrained marginal modes can be impossible. Locked and independent chains share the same state marginals but different path entropy. The prefix inspector separates filtering and prefix-only forecasts from hindsight; the original unsmoothed Baum-Welch trainer retains its fixed synthetic controls and scores. Current checks: 58 Python tests, 10 Node tests with actual Chrome, independent tiny-path references, a standalone rational-report verifier and isolated installed replay. Probabilities and path objectives are exact in the rational workbench; entropy is approximate and model-conditional, not calibrated uncertainty, true-state recovery or a quality ranking. [Equations and limits](https://github.com/midya8/chainlab/blob/main/PATHS.md).
  - Case study: [chainlab.md](chainlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/chainlab](https://github.com/midya8/chainlab)
- **KernelLab** - Exact Gaussian-process regression and finite-grid expected improvement built from scratch in pure Python — standard GP theory, no new method: an isotropic squared-exponential covariance with a fixed supplied constant mean and iid Gaussian observation noise, five explicit parameters (length_scale, signal_variance, noise_variance, jitter, prior_mean — signal and noise are variances, not standard deviations), conditioned by Cholesky with triangular solves; production code never inverts a dense matrix, and a Gauss-Jordan inverse exists only in tests as an independent tiny reference. The training covariance is K+(noise+jitter)I; nonpositive pivots fail — no adaptive jitter, PSD projection, parameter normalization or silent retry — and tiny negative posterior diagonals within max(1e-12, signal_variance*1e-10) are clamped and counted while material negatives fail. An analytic one-point posterior checks mean, latent variance, LOO and log marginal likelihood, and LOO is a row-level conditional diagnostic with fixed assumptions: d_i=(A^-1)_ii from the squared norm of L^-1 e_i, mean y_i−alpha_i/d_i, effective observation variance 1/d_i. Demo on a seed-19 fixture (15 training inputs in [−3,3], truth sin(1.5x)+0.15x, Gaussian noise SD .12; 101 noisy test observations): length .8 gives observation RMSE .6242366399, mean NLL .1040604253 and 100/101 pointwise 2-SD coverage, while length 5 gives 1.0038088074, 12.1309060441 and 22/101, against an unconditioned .7879412723/1.2321062355 — the wide-kernel adverse arm is kept visible, not tuned away, and the fixture is constructed to need shorter-scale variation, not an untouched benchmark. Training LML −7.418945/−210.975199 and LOO mean NLL .069448/15.090203 are reported without selection. `suggest` is deterministic maximization only and requires zero observation noise: EI=delta·Phi(delta/sigma)+sigma·phi(delta/sigma), with the exact deterministic limit max(delta,0) at zero variance; exact binary64 matches to training rows are excluded, duplicates are errors, and an all-known pool returns status "exhausted" with a null suggestion. An offline optimization demo on sin(2x)+0.4cos(5x)−0.05x² over an 81-point grid gives, at nine evaluations each, best observed 1.045544 at x=−2.5 and finite-grid simple regret 0.000163 for EI, against 0.972762/0.072944 for farthest-point space filling and 0.646233/0.399473 for one seeded uniform random control (grid oracle 1.045706 at x=−2.4) — one objective, one seed, no parameter sweep and no general speedup claim. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, 43 Python tests and four Node/Chrome tests, fit/predict/response JSON CLI, reproducible SVG and Python 3.10–3.13 CI. Live v0.2 adds a [target-response inspector](https://midya8.github.io/kernellab/) that keeps both original GP models fixed: edit one observed target and inspect all 15 signed response weights — the conditional mean moves while latent/observation variances stay unchanged. Independent browser LDL versus native Cholesky checks cover 202 query witnesses and 3,030 direct edited-target predictions, plus high-precision tiny controls and shared-axis endpoint checks; original misspecification results and optimization traces are unchanged. Signed weights are not causal attribution or calibrated confidence, and refitting invalidates the fixed-kernel contrast. The installed wheel reproduces eight modules and seven commands. [Derivation and numerical limits](https://github.com/midya8/kernellab/blob/main/RESPONSE.md).
  - Case study: [kernellab.md](kernellab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/kernellab](https://github.com/midya8/kernellab)
- **BoostLab** - Standard gradient boosting built from scratch in pure Python — no new theory: binary Bernoulli classification on finite numeric rows, a logit plus a stable sigmoid, stable softplus loss, per-row gradients y − sigmoid(z) checked by central finite differences, and a training-only intercept from half-count smoothed prevalence. Friedman (2001), Greedy Function Approximation: A Gradient Boosting Machine (https://doi.org/10.1214/aos/1013203451), supplies the gradient-boosting reference, and the gbm guide describes the Bernoulli Newton ratio used here with explicit L2 and a cap. Each round fits a greedy squared-error regression tree to the current residuals with fixed shrinkage at rate 0.5; default leaves are mean residuals in [−1,1], and an optional Newton refinement replaces each leaf with sum(y−p)/(sum(p(1−p))+L2) with the unshrunk step capped (L2 in [0,1000000], default 0; cap in (0,10], default 3) — the split objective stays residual-SSE, so this is an educational variant, not a clone of gbm/sklearn/XGBoost. Only training rows and labels determine the intercept, residuals, splits and leaf values; validation logloss picks the earliest prefix improving by over 1e-12, including round zero, and the API never accepts test data — a separate demo reports test metrics only after fitting. Demo on seed-7 256/128/512 rows (class is the sign of the coordinate product, flipped with probability 0.05): stumps keep round zero at .4980 accuracy/.7060 logloss, while their unselected 80-round checkpoint reaches .6367/.7366 at 240 nodes; the depth-2 selected 80-round model has 556 nodes at .8906/.3400; the Newton depth-2 arm (L2=1, cap 3) has validation select round 35 with 237 nodes at .900390625/.3341242425, and its last round 80 sits at .8984375/.3468631810 with 538 nodes; training/validation logloss is .2487/.3457 for the mean depth-2 model and .1776606433/.3379099355 for the Newton model at its selected round 35 — the setting was not chosen from a Newton grid search, the fixture is development data already seen, and the comparison does not isolate a causal curvature or regularization benefit. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, 36 Python tests and five Node/Chrome tests, train/predict/path JSON CLI/API, offline demo and Python 3.10–3.13 CI. Version 0.2 adds a live training-path inspector ([Try the training path](https://midya8.github.io/boostlab/) · [Derivation and numerical limits](https://github.com/midya8/boostlab/blob/main/PATH.md)): all three original arms retain all 80 fitted trees even when validation selects zero, 80 or 35, so the inspected prefix is separated from the original selection and selected/last logits are shown for the same input, with all 243 train/validation/report-only-test metric states on a common zero-based loss axis. Editing either coordinate clears the historical fixture label. Every tree route, included/omitted term and complete model can be inspected and exported, and no fitting, selection, model API or uploads run on the page. The native path and an independent browser implementation agree on 4,860 original sample/prefix states and 1,458 edited states, with 78 full route witnesses; independent Fraction sums and recursive scalar metric calculations check all 243 metric states. Actual Chrome checks every original state, 180 UI configurations, physical keyboard input, invalid/stale witness refusal, clipboard failures/success and JSON Blobs; 320/390/1280 layouts and expanded mobile routes/JSON are tested and visually inspected. The installed wheel imports all seven modules, executes all five main commands and reproduces all three original fits, histories and witnesses outside checkout. The original 29 tests remain within the 36 Python tests, and the five Node/Chrome tests add independent inference and UI coverage. The unchanged fitting implementation updates logits incrementally and archived prefix metrics use the existing initial + rate*math.fsum(leaves) inference; browser compensation agrees within checked numerical tolerances, not bitwise or exact-real guarantees; near-zero threshold labels can depend on rounding; algebraic tree terms are neither feature attribution nor causal explanations; sigmoid is not calibrated confidence; same round budget is not equal capacity; Newton also changes regularization/capping; and the development fixture was inspected.
  - Case study: [boostlab.md](boostlab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/boostlab](https://github.com/midya8/boostlab)
- **ShapleyLab** - Exact small-feature Shapley attribution built from scratch in pure Python: the standard Shapley/SHAP formula over an empirical-background replacement game — no new theory — with every coalition enumerated (2^d values by integer bit mask) and available for audit, a scalar score callback API and a strict JSON CLI. Demo on a product scorer at x=(2,3,99) with prediction 6: a zero background gives [3,3,0] but a shifted background gives [2,3,0] — the reference choice changes the question, not the model prediction; jointly sampled missing features preserve their mutual dependence, and a diagonal-background counterexample has zero attribution and zero efficiency residual while singleton hybrids sit off the observed manifold, so additivity does not certify explanation validity. Calls are budgeted up front ((2^d − 1)·B + 1; 3 features/2 background rows require 15 calls), and an independent factorial-ordering reference checks dimensions 1–6 alongside linearity, symmetry, dummy and feature-permutation tests. Optional atomic groups move correlated blocks together as single players — a three-way product gives grouped [1/2, 1/2], not the post-hoc [2/3, 1/3] from summing individual values — up to 64 raw features and 12 groups, with no within-group credit split reported and no cross-group dependence guarantee. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, 49 Python tests and four Node/actual-Chrome tests, JSON CLI/API, offline demo and Python 3.10–3.13 CI. Optional seeded permutation mode handles up to 64 individual features with cached coalitions and descriptive Monte Carlo standard errors — a two-permutation product gives [0.5, 0.5, 0] despite exact [1/3, 1/3, 1/3], so zero observed Monte Carlo error is not an accuracy bound — with no confidence guarantee and no sampled groups. v0.2 adds a live replacement and permutation ledger: the inspector exposes every background replacement row, coalition value, weighted predecessor term and complete small player ordering; native audits log actual calls to the original exact engine and cached paths add no score evaluations; the independent browser recomputes seven fixed public synthetic games under bounded target edits, not a user callback; atomic grouped credits stay distinct from post-summed individual credits. 49 Python tests plus four Node/actual-Chrome tests check 95 coordinate edits, 128 Fraction games, 64 independent BigInt product games, all 44 original coalitions, 18 players and 30 orderings; the installed 11-module/four-command reproduction retains the original exact/grouped/sampled modes and all prior demo reports; signed zero-centered bars, hybrid provenance, invalid-state clearing and JSON witnesses are checked at 320/390/1280 widths. The original diagonal-reference squared-difference game still assigns zero credits through unrealistic singleton hybrids, and the P=2 sample still assigns zero estimated error to a wrong dummy-looking credit — efficiency alone validates neither; no new model, training, causal attribution, representative-background, confidence or explanation-quality claim. v0.3 adds a two-level Owen mode: the same three-way product can assign [1/3, 1/3, 1/3] to individual features, [1/2, 1/2] to two atomic groups, and [1/4, 1/4, 1/2] to Owen members when a, b must arrive together — one score and background, different allowed orders, so equal splitting and post-summing ordinary credits are not the same rule. The mode keeps every background-replacement evaluation, member coefficient, quotient-group marginal and complete admissible ordering; its native checker imports no generator and independently replays the declared synthetic score and full allocation report, while the independent browser computes permutation-count weights, lets you edit the supplied model and partition, and downloads evidence the native CLI can verify. 72 Python tests include 556 signed game/partition cases checked across 50,362 independent Fraction orderings, 14,668 unanimity-support/partition cases and separate primitive-mutation checks; 132 complete native/JavaScript reports agree, plus 206 broad-magnitude, half-even, subnormal and cancellation reports independently native-verify, and an original BigInt binary64-unit summation helper fixes a pre-release double-rounding failure without widening tolerance. Actual Chrome checks all 12 original controls at 320/390/1280, 12 trusted completed native-verified downloads, and raw/sigmoid six-feature/32-background requests with 2017 evaluations and 720 paths each; fresh installed-wheel checks preserve all 95 prior edit archives and original modes, all 24 feature/PR checks, 12 main checks and three Pages jobs passed, and all 45 deployed browser states match the tested unmasked pixels. Established coalitional-value theory with original supplied synthetic controls; no new allocation theorem or measured explanation-quality result, and standalone supplied tables support up to eight features while full declared-score/browser reports cover up to six features and 32 background rows. [Live editable Owen inspector](https://midya8.github.io/shapleylab/owen.html) · [Method, sources and boundaries](https://github.com/midya8/shapleylab/blob/main/OWEN.md) · [Reviewed change](https://github.com/midya8/shapleylab/pull/2). [Derivation, six sources and limits](https://github.com/midya8/shapleylab/blob/main/LEDGER.md).
The case study now frames the four modes as one choice to make, not four features to list: individual Shapley, atomic groups, two-level Owen, and seeded permutation sampling all run on the same declared background, and they answer different allocation questions — none of them a causal one. The figures the entry hadn’t carried yet: at P=4096, seed 0, the sampled three-way product lands at [0.3220215, 0.3415527, 0.3364258] with standard errors around [0.0073017, 0.0074108, 0.0073835] in eight calls with caching, against the exact [1/3, 1/3, 1/3]; a 64-feature sum sampled at P=32, seed 4 uses 2002 calls against a 2018 preflight bound and returns 64 unit attributions with zero Monte Carlo errors, because every linear marginal is constant. Grouping is cheaper because the game is smaller: the three-way grouped fixture uses 4 calls instead of 8, and a tested 64-feature, eight-group sum uses 256 calls with one background row and returns eight values of 8. The limits are stated up front — individual mode takes 1–12 features, backgrounds run 1–1000 rows, the call budget runs 1–1,000,000 with a 200,000 default, validated before the callback runs — and the Owen release checks ten deployed assets against public source alongside the 45 deployed browser states.
  - Case study: [shapleylab.md](shapleylab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/shapleylab](https://github.com/midya8/shapleylab)
- **PolicyLens** - Offline policy evaluation for logged contextual bandits built from scratch in pure Python: standard IPS, SNIPS, DM and DR estimators — no new statistical method — with strict schema validation, explicit support and overlap diagnostics (ESS, max/mean/zero-weight fraction) and clipping trade-offs labeled, not hidden. Demo on a constructed two-context design with known target value 0.78: twenty deterministic mean-reward rows reproduce IPS/SNIPS/DR = 0.78 against a wrong direct estimate of 0.5, and clipping at 1 moves IPS to 0.185, SNIPS to 0.6166667 and DR to 0.535 while ESS rises 4.9231 to 8.4706; a 2000-row seed-13 Bernoulli log with oracle q gives DR = 0.7532475, not exact population truth — selected development fixtures, not a power/variance study. An optional crossfit command fits held-out categorical reward means (five folds of 400 rows each, seed 17, fixed prior): direct 0.7496455 and DR 0.7509088 on the same noisy log, explicitly not closer to truth than oracle DR 0.7532475 and not an estimator ranking; row-iid splits do not protect clustered/time data. Target-positive/behavior-zero actions get a no-nonparametric-identification label instead of a silent zero-weight success. The v0.2 live row-contribution ledger exposes every observed propensity, raw/used weight, direct prediction, signed residual, IPS/DM/DR additive term, normalized SNIPS term, unsupported target action and signed cap removal across five fixed synthetic cases, with an independent browser evaluator checking bounded target-mixture and single-row reward edits against native Python witnesses; the signed shared-axis cap path keeps negative DR visible, delete-one-row reports hold policy and predictions fixed, and zero observed target mass leaves SNIPS undefined rather than substituting a value. The original 28 tests cover the core estimators; the current suite has 36 Python and four Node/actual-Chrome tests, including 160 independent Fraction cases, 80 independent integer-grid browser-oracle cases, 95 native/browser edit witnesses and 589 audited rows plus 589 deletion reports, with installed-wheel checks importing seven modules and running all five commands outside checkout. Browser regressions also check that the estimate text fits its cards across all five cases at 320, 390, 768 and 1280 pixels. Original four reports, source data and estimator implementations are unchanged. Cap paths and ESS are descriptive, not confidence, tuning or deployment advice. Dependency-free Python 3.10+, JSON CLI/API, offline demo and Python 3.10-3.13 CI. [Live row ledger](https://midya8.github.io/policylens/). [Equations and limits](https://github.com/midya8/policylens/blob/main/LEDGER.md).
  - Case study: [policylens.md](policylens.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/policylens](https://github.com/midya8/policylens)
- **DriftLens** - A dependency-free Python CLI and API for standard RBF MMD two-sample tests, with a separate exact supplied-Gram kernel-family mode alongside the floating RBF side. The RBF statistic is an unbiased squared MMD over one cached pooled kernel, re-indexed for permutations, with exact tiny-sample enumeration and seeded Monte Carlo permutations with nonzero finite p-values; bandwidth and preprocessing are prespecified, never tuned against group separation on the same p-value. Demo on seed-13 30x4 standard-normal samples: same-generator MMD squared -0.004871, p=0.55; adding +2 to each current coordinate gives 0.634539, p=0.002 at the 1/500 resolution floor — selected development fixtures, not a power or type-I error study. A tiny separated 2-vs-2 example has exact p=1/3 over six allocations. Prespecified bandwidth families get Holm step-down adjusted p-values: bandwidths [1, 2, 4] on the shifted demo each have raw p=0.002 and Holm p=0.006, and a tiny exact example with raw p=1/3 does not reject at alpha=0.4 after Holm even though both raw p-values pass. Prespecified within-block permutations test a different conditional null from unrestricted label permutations: on a constructed mixture, pooled MMD squared is 0.648886 under both schemes while unrestricted p=0.014 and blocked p=1.000, over exact spaces of 184,756 and 2,025 allocations, because the features are identical within each stratum — the blocked result is not universally more correct, and neither scheme diagnoses a cause. Descriptive witness diagnostics report the highest-absolute-score rows per group without changing any p-value or statistic, and imbalanced-group scoring reuses cached row sums: in a 2-vs-18 exact demo, 190 allocations each read 1 kernel pair plus 2 cached row sums after 380 setup reads, where direct scoring would read 190 kernel pairs per allocation — operation counts, not wall time. The exact supplied-Gram mode enumerates every allowed allocation over 4-10 pooled rows and 1-6 supplied kernels, at most 252 allocations, computing every diagonal-excluding MMD² and tied upper-tail rank and recalibrating the smallest rank over the same shared orbit: in a six-row, three-view development control at alpha 1/5, the uncorrected smallest raw p rejects 8/20 allowed allocations, the global test 4/20 and Holm-any 0/20 — complete finite-orbit counts, not measured real-world false-positive rates or a power benchmark. 58 Python tests and four Node tests cover both modes, with CI on Python 3.10-3.13; the installed wheel is checked outside the checkout, all fourteen module origins, eleven controls through both installed entry points and twenty-two forged-report CLI rejections, and the browser suite checks eleven controls at 320/390/1280 with completed native-verified downloads and the ten-row/six-kernel/252-allocation boundary. No new test, theorem or benchmark-superiority claim; non-rejection is not equivalence, and rejection does not identify a cause or a model-quality loss. [Live exact-orbit inspector](https://midya8.github.io/driftlens/) · [Method, nine sources and limits](https://github.com/midya8/driftlens/blob/main/ORBIT.md) · [Merged implementation](https://github.com/midya8/driftlens/pull/1).
  - Case study: [driftlens.md](driftlens.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/driftlens](https://github.com/midya8/driftlens)
- **SetGuard** - Turn fixed classifier probabilities and separate calibration labels into split-conformal prediction sets. The inclusion ledger shows all score ranks, equality ties, rare/absent-class infinity thresholds and candidate-label decisions for pooled and classwise modes. Five synthetic cases and 19 sampled alpha points give 95 bounded witnesses; the deletion ledger checks deterministic rank arithmetic, not exchangeability. The original 200-calibration/2000-test-row example keeps pooled coverage .9235 at mean set size 3.017 and deliberate adverse-shift coverage .076 visible. Classwise coverage .9285 and mean size 3.1095 do not imply an improvement on every label: class d realizes .8859. 41 Python/four Node-Chrome tests and isolated installed-wheel reproduction retain the original method and six CLI commands. Marginal/classwise validity averages over calibration/test randomness under its assumptions; no input-conditional, fixed-split or deployment guarantee. [Case study](setguard.md) · [Source](https://github.com/midya8/setguard) · [Live ledger](https://midya8.github.io/setguard/).

- **CalibrLab** - Confidence calibration built from scratch in pure Python: ECE/MCE/Brier/NLL metrics, per-bin reliability tables, and temperature scaling fit on validation NLL only, measured on an overfit small-data MLP whose ECE falls 0.2765 to 0.0651 at exactly zero accuracy change, with the oracle gap (0.0076 NLL) reported. 56 Python tests and four Node/actual-Chrome tests, with CI running Python 3.10-3.13. v0.2 adds a live score ledger: a native API/CLI and an independent browser evaluator expose stable row logit NLL, class Brier terms, every signed bin gap/ECE contribution, before-to-selected bin migration and all fixed-temperature row deletions, for 2..20 bins or T 0.1..20, while the validation fit remains the original 191-point NLL grid, never a test fit, and a 19-point descriptive path stays separate from the optimizer; full input/report JSON can be copied or downloaded with the input staying in the page. Five public synthetic controls retain all 240 validation/480 test logits and the unchanged original report — the adverse caller example, uniform ties, within-bin cancellation and singleton underflow, the latter shared-value arithmetic examples rather than independent evaluation samples. The suite verifies 95 full native/browser witnesses, 9,367 row/deletion records, 55,328 class Brier terms, 160 independent 70-digit Decimal games and 80 integer/uniform controls, with actual UI checks visiting all 493 positions/50 bins, and an installed wheel rebuilds the original full training/logits/report and all 95 witnesses outside the checkout, importing 7 modules and running 5 commands. Limits stay explicit: standard temperature scaling and proper losses, not a new calibration theorem; ECE/MCE depend on bins, finite counts and only top-class probabilities; lower NLL/Brier does not isolate calibration; fixed-temperature deletion is not a validation refit, causal effect or uncertainty interval; positive scaling preserves real-arithmetic argmax while binary64 rounding/underflow limits are explicit; the original rounded-NLL subtraction gives the .0076 oracle gap while unchanged full precision .007543134492483228 rounds to .0075 in the new ledger; no stronger deployment guarantee. [Live score ledger](https://midya8.github.io/calibrlab/) · [Equations/schema/limits](https://github.com/midya8/calibrlab/blob/main/LEDGER.md)

- **DistillLab** - Knowledge distillation built from scratch in pure Python: a teacher trained on abundant data, a 10x smaller student trained on a tenth of the data, and the measured gain compared against matched control arms — pseudo-labels, soft-label distillation, label smoothing, and a permuted-target control at exactly chance. On the synthetic fixture the distilled student scores 0.7469 test accuracy, versus 0.6813 for direct labels and 0.7094 for teacher argmax labels — matched-arm results, not proof of what caused the difference. The +0.0656 gain over direct breaks into +0.0281 from pseudo-labels over direct and a further +0.0375 from soft targets over pseudo-labels, against a teacher at 0.8344; the permuted-target control sits at exactly chance (0.1250) and one uniform-smoothing setting (eps=0.1) scores 0.6781, below direct. The distilled student also memorizes less — train 0.8000 with a 0.0531 train-test gap, against direct's 1.0000 and 0.3187 — its distribution sits closer to the teacher's (KL 0.9563 vs 1.0932), and T=4 adds +0.0250 over the T=1 ablation at 0.7219. The current suite has 36 Python tests and seven substantive Node/real-Chrome tests; the earlier step-inspector release had 24 Python tests and four Node/actual-Chrome tests. v0.2 adds a live student-update inspector: select any original trained student arm or its original initialization, one of the 80 student-training rows, temperature, soft weight and step size, and independent Python and browser evaluators expose hard/soft class derivatives, every simultaneous parameter update, hidden activations, stable CE/entropy/KL terms and all original 80 training/320 test predictions, with full input/report JSON; each entered state starts from its chosen frozen model and test labels never enter the gradient. A supplied two-hidden-unit control makes the finite-step limitation visible: selected-row loss rises from 1.3466 to 3.6897 at eta=2 although the local directional derivative is negative. The suite verifies 36 complete native/browser witnesses with 8,900 parameter records and 14,001 evaluation positions, 160 independent 70-digit Decimal logit controls, 80 uniform-class controls, every-parameter finite differences on the original initialization and the overshoot network, and 21 original backprop/update states, and an installed wheel rebuilds the original teacher and six student fits, full data/logits/report and all witnesses outside the checkout, importing seven modules and running all four commands. The new inspector uses stable, unclipped logit-space objectives, while the original helper functions clamp probabilities at 1e-12 and their gradient formula is the ordinary unclipped derivative, the distinction below that floor explicit; T changes target entropy and objective scale, so loss values across different T do not rank generalization; a lower local loss is not a generalization, calibration, causal-mechanism or deployment guarantee, and one step is not a training run. [Live student-update inspector](https://midya8.github.io/distilllab/) · [Equations, schema and numerical limits](https://github.com/midya8/distilllab/blob/main/STEP.md)
  - v0.3 adds a separate target/rest KL chain-rule workbench: four teacher tails preserve the supplied-label target/rest mass at the selected temperature, while independent native analytic and browser reverse-mode gradients expose every class derivative and parameter update. It includes the CE/KL teacher-entropy offset, true excluded-class normalization, bounded input and full replay archives. 36 Python tests retain all 24 earlier tests; seven substantive Node/Chrome tests (eight runner entries), 28 witnesses, 112 variants, 2,368 centered finite-difference checks and both installed CLI entrypoints pass. No DKD training, new method, generalization result or causal account of the original gain is claimed. [Target/rest workbench](https://midya8.github.io/distilllab/tail.html) · [Equations and replay contract](https://github.com/midya8/distilllab/blob/main/TAIL.md)

- **LoRALab** - Exact predictive-rank certificates now separate width, product rank, all-input softmax quotient rank and finite-probe rank. Independent Fraction/BigInt engines, a separate Bareiss verifier, constructive factors/nonzero minors and explicit blind directions; all 124 original states,39 Python/seven Node tests, complete report hashes and installed replay. Exact real linear output probabilities only, not rounded runtime, storage savings or training. [Exact inspector](https://midya8.github.io/loralab/rank.html). Earlier coordinate inspector retained:  Same model, different factor-SGD step: equivalent factor coordinates (`B' = BC`, `A' = C^-1 A`) preserve `BA` in real arithmetic, but ordinary factor SGD need not preserve the next merged model — the inspector exposes the full finite-step second-order identity `delta(BA) = -eta_A BB^T G - eta_B GA^T A + eta_A eta_B G(BA)^T G`, and scalar compensation `eta_A' = eta/c^2`, `eta_B' = eta*c^2` reproduces the original next merged model in real arithmetic, a coordinate identity rather than a new optimizer. At width 2, epoch 30, first example, learning rate 0.1, scale 4, the ordinary next-matrix difference is 0.0056767304 while scalar compensation gives residual 0 in binary64. All 124 original epoch boundaries (widths 1/2/4/8) remain inspectable with full matrix gauges, 31-scale sweeps, per-example outputs and complete report/input/archive exports, with no best-state selection or retraining from the controls. Original results preserved: base B accuracy 0.4444; width-2 B 1.0000 with 67.7% of full fine-tuning's parameters but retention 0.7556 versus 0.9556; widths 4/8 cost more parameters than full fine-tuning; three output rows cap update-matrix rank at three; merge-back diff ~1.07e-14 below the unchanged 1e-12 gate. 31 Python tests (all 14 original intact) and four Node/actual-Chrome tests; 54 complete native/browser witnesses with 591,615 numeric values, 80-digit Decimal checks, installed-wheel reproduction (eight modules, four commands), seven CI jobs. Synthetic finite classifier only — no claim about transformers, Adam, unseen tasks or the cause of retention loss.
  - Case study: [loralab.md](loralab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/loralab](https://github.com/midya8/loralab)
- **SpecLab** - Speculative decoding built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a speedup number and a counted verification cost inspectable, on supplied finite draft/target tables where every acceptance, law and cost is exact, not a claim about real-model latency. The v0.4 extension decides while drafting, rather than choosing a complete block length before the round: the safe before-next max(q) rule and the sampled-token keep-and-verify rule preserve the full target law, while the deliberately wrong sample-censoring control gives (5/8,3/8) instead of the target (1/2,1/2), with complete two-position sequence TV 1/8. Every stopped leaf, acceptance/residual/bonus branch and joint sequence/count outcome is retained, and a separate verifier enumerates full maximum-length paths, marginalizes unused suffixes and expands decoding states forward; the browser recomputes complete edited conditional tables in BigInt, invalidates stale evidence and downloads reports for independent replay. 61 Python tests retain all 47 earlier tests, with 14 new tests covering 125 binary distribution/threshold controls, seeded prefix-dependent tables, every scalar-leaf tamper and full count conservation; four Node/Chrome tests compare full reports and exercise 12 controls, 604 stopped leaves, 1,503 verification branches, 1,950 exact sequence cells, 36 layouts, trusted edits and actual downloaded evidence. Target-only and every fixed cap 1-3 are full-strength baselines, and unused before-next distribution lookups are charged. The scope is supplied exact finite tables only — 1-3 tokens, horizon 1-4, max draft length 1-3 — with no EOS, learned predictor, entropy approximation, library default claim, measured latency or new-algorithm claim, and stopping changes operation counts, not automatically hardware speed. The derivation and six primary sources are in the source STOPPING.md; the negative control is defined here, not attributed to a production library. The v0.3 extension adds an exact oracle lookahead planner: from each emitted prefix it chooses a block length before drafting, using fully known finite target/draft tables, retaining every Bellman action's immediate/future costs, transitions and nonnegative slack, then separately composing complete policy sequence/count laws; no-draft action 0 and every fixed gamma 1..3 are full-strength baselines, and ties select the smallest action. On the switching four-token fixture the optimal assigned cost is 23/10 versus best fixed 47/20, an exact gain of 1/20 hypothetical units, while charging target positions instead of verification rounds selects no drafting everywhere; the full target law remains AAAA/BBBB with mass 1/2 each, only empty, BB and BBB are reached as policy round starts, and unreachable prefix values stay inspectable rather than charged as executed work. The 47 tests include all 12 tiny prefix policies on 25 model pairs under three objectives, 72 prefix-dependent full joint-law comparisons and 36 size/horizon/gamma-limit cases; the 115-cell SVG embeds the complete report, CI checks seven modes from a 12-module installed wheel outside checkout, and the original greedy and v0.2 stochastic demos are preserved. The v0.2 extension adds an exact stochastic finite-table engine: draft tokens are sampled, accepted with min(1,p/q), and the first rejected token is replaced with the normalized positive residual; full proposal/acceptance/rejection/bonus certificates support complete sequence laws and joint sequence/count laws, and a separate procedural sampler uses exact integer RNG tickets. The corrected four-token law has exact TV zero while using the target directly as a rejection fallback has TV 141/1024 on the prefix-dependent fixture; its expected 75/32 modeled verification rounds require 179/32 target positions and 13/4 draft calls. The 34 tests cover both paths, including all 2,187 binary prefix-table pairs against an independent kernel oracle, all 225 ternary quarter-grid distribution pairs, 36 vocabulary/horizon/lookahead configurations and every procedural RNG ticket for all 25 binary quarter-grid pairs at horizon 2, with both output and cost laws matching the analytical engine; the figure binds all 96 data cells and full witness metadata, and five CI jobs cover Python 3.10-3.13, saved full JSON/SVG parity and all five CLI modes from an installed ten-module wheel outside checkout. The original greedy n-gram path is unchanged: a cheap draft proposes a block, the target verifies it, and a greedy-construction invariant keeps the output identical to target-only decoding; the 14 tests cover implementation equivalence across four draft/target configurations, three generation lengths and gamma 1/2/4/8, both counting contracts, acceptance bounds and consistency, pinned fixture numbers, determinism, both sides of every CLI gate and the low-acceptance negative control. On the fixture (8 prompts x 60 tokens, gamma=4) the output is identical on every prompt with mean acceptance 0.800, 122 vs 480 verification rounds — a 74.6% modeled reduction — while actual sequential scoring work is 510 vs 480 calls, reported plainly; a mismatched order-2/order-3 pair drops acceptance to 0.03 with savings nearly gone. The scope is supplied finite tables only — vocabulary 1..3, horizon 1..4, gamma 1..3, no EOS, real model, GPU kernel or throughput benchmark — with planning and policy-lookup overhead unpriced, assigned parallel-round and position-score costs hypothetical rather than measured latency or FLOPs, and the same probability law not meaning identical output under a shared seed; the planner is not an online confidence/entropy stopping rule, a trained acceptance head, a measured latency predictor or a new adaptive-decoding algorithm, and the comparison is descriptive under declared costs, not a real-model throughput result or causal component isolation. Prior adaptive-lookahead work is attributed, with the state/action contract, Bellman proof and related work in the source LOOKAHEAD.md and the proof, schema, source distinctions and counting contract in STOCHASTIC.md; no novelty claim.
  - Case study: [speclab.md](speclab.md)

- **StructGate** - Full-model constrained decoding with support-aware KL and exact finite-language conditioning. The v0.3 DFA extension prices the future instead of only listing the legal set: the default word 01 has source probability 1/20, viability-mask probability 1/2 and global conditional probability 1/10, because exact conditioning weights each next symbol by p(next) h(next)/h(prefix) - the source probability that a future suffix completes an accepted word - while local masking only renormalizes to p/Z. A legal next symbol can still have an unlikely future, and stranded mass stays in a failure sink that full total variation must include. 64 Python tests retain all 54 previous tests and six Node/actual-Chrome tests retain all three previous, covering 60 seeded small DFAs, 24 complete reports, 2,043 future rows, 6,550 symbol rows and 2,239 marginal rows, with Chrome checking all 156 selected-word prefixes; the 32-symbol ending control represents 4,294,967,296 possible source words with 192 transition rows. QuantLab 0.3 traces activation x weight integer dot products, not only packed weights. The constructed default has zero weight error, yet activation rounding changes a strict row-1 winner into a zero-code tie won by row 0: W=[[127,0],[0,127]], x=[0.4,0.49], activation range 127, stored scales exactly 1; exact-real scores are 127 times the represented inputs with row 1 strictly larger, while quantized activations [0,0] make every product and accumulator 0. The current suite has 47 Python tests, including all 39 earlier tests; independent floor/remainder rounding checks 240 seeded reports and all 519 signed half-unit controls; Python Fraction and independent browser BigInt dyadics agree on 72 complete reports with 6,848 products and all exact error strings; actual NumPy 2.2.6 int32 matmul and cumsum check every accumulator and prefix. - **PlanBench** - Validation and deterministic simulation of agent task plans: semantic DAG checks, seeded flaky-tool simulation with retries and skip cascades, makespan/cost/critical-path/parallelism metrics and CI gates. PlanBench now also computes the exact finite retry-risk law for a supplied DAG: joint plan-success, completion-time and cost probabilities over declared failure rates, durations and costs, including exhausted retries, skip cascades and shared ancestors sampled once. 39 Python tests retain all 29 originals, plus four Node/actual-Chrome tests.

A cheap failed run can satisfy a budget by skipping the work: within-limit probability is not successful-completion probability. A half-failing prerequisite followed by a five-time-unit dependent step has probability 1/2 of fitting time1/cost1 but zero probability of succeeding within those limits, and for two independent half-failing parallel steps with one retry each the expected makespan is 7/4 rather than the maximum individual expected duration 3/2. The browser enumerates all potential terminal assignments, schedules completion events and sums unused skipped-step outcomes; complete histories retain final-attempt starts, dependency-ready times, costs and unconditional/conditional summaries, and zero-success conditional expectations stay undefined. Four Node/actual-Chrome tests compare 28 complete Fraction/BigInt reports, 260 terminal histories, 177 joint atoms, 778 selected step rows, 24 responsive views and native-replayed trusted exports; an independent Bernoulli-bit/completion-event oracle checks 65 seeded DAGs and retry boundaries, and an installed wheel reproduces both new CLI entrypoints and the original validate/simulate gates outside the checkout. At most six steps and two retries each; calls fail independently with unlimited workers and immediate retries, so resource contention, backoff, timeouts and correlated outages are excluded, and exact rational laws are not production reliability, empirical confidence or a new scheduling algorithm. The original seeded simulator stays unchanged.

- **SubgameLab** - Exact finite sequential multiagent analysis built from scratch in pure Python — it makes the difference between a plan that is stable at the root and a plan that stays credible in every subgame inspectable, on supplied small games where every continuation payoff, reach probability and full-player alternative is an exact rational, not a claim about real-agent strength. In the developed default, P1 stopping pays (1, 4) while entering lets P2 choose (-1, 0) or (3, 2): complete plans 00 and 11 are both pure Nash, because P2's unreached fight cannot improve its root payoff, but only 11 is subgame perfect — if P2's node is actually reached, switching gains 2. The same-player control shows the gap from the other side: P1 gains 1 by stopping at the root, 0 by entering then stopping, and 3 by entering then continuing, so plan 00 is stable against changing either node alone yet a joint two-node change gains 2 and it is not Nash; the browser calls the weaker property root one-node stability rather than letting it borrow the Nash name. A chance control reaches terminal (2, 2) with probability 1 while its probability-0 branch still lets P2 choose (0, 0) or (0, 5): both complete plans are root Nash with identical root outcomes, and only the latter is subgame perfect — zero reach does not erase the subgame, because subtree values are evaluated from the subtree root with no division by its zero reach probability. Ties need whole continuation plans: when both P2 actions pay P2 3 while P1 receives 1 or 4, and P1's outside option is 2, the retained continuation decides whether P1 should stop or enter, and both subgame-perfect plans survive; the all-zero three-decision control retains all 8 complete plans. Backward induction combines every pair of child-subgame equilibrium plans before selecting every maximizing current action and never takes coordinatewise maxima from incompatible continuation plans. The independent verifier imports validation only: it enumerates terminal paths, multiplies chance/choice indicators, sums exact terminal payoffs and enumerates every full unilateral assignment in every subtree, reconstructing the complete report and each node's equilibrium-plan correspondence without generator recurrence or backward induction, and strict recursive comparison rejects extra, missing, reordered, mistyped or forged evidence. The separate JavaScript engine uses BigInt rational arithmetic and matches every complete report; its browser replay uses the same engine and is explicitly not an independent verifier. Actual Nashpy 0.0.43 builds a fresh normal-form matrix for each subtree directly from input paths and checks every pure strategy pair with its best-response API — 533 input-built subtree matrices and 2,353 pure-pair checks covering all 1,233 profile classifications — with exact rational entries scaled to integers, the largest absolute scaled entry 18 and every nonzero payoff difference at least 1, and a documented caveat that Nashpy uses NumPy isclose, so this is supplemental numerical reference evidence, not exact certification; Gambit was researched but its local build did not finish, so no Gambit validation is claimed. The evidence is checked, not asserted: 14 Python tests include an exhaustive class of all 2,916 two-decision games — four player-owner patterns × 729 ternary six-payoff assignments — and 65 complete Python/BigInt reports span ten developed controls and 55 seeded trees, retaining 1,233 profiles, 12,820 subgame/player audits and 48,250 full-player alternatives; both engines reject 317 noninput forgeries each; the installed wheel replays five site-packages module origins, ten controls, two entrypoints and 20 CLI forgery failures; actual Chrome checks 40 views at 1440/768/390/320 with 388 plan selections, 1,209 continuation selections and 2,837 alternative rows, and ten trusted inputs and ten certificates download and replay through the native CLI. PR1 merged after all 12 latest feature/PR jobs passed; main 63cea0d5 retains the initial commit and feature history, all 27 public files match the tested source, and all 48 unmasked deployed captures match local pixels. The scope stays narrow — two players, at most six binary player decisions, two binary chance nodes and 17 nodes, complete pure contingent plans with singleton information sets and exact canonical rational inputs with numerator/denominator at most 1,000 — with no hidden information, mixed-equilibrium enumeration, imperfect recall, CFR, learning, preference inference, formal verification, new theorem, scalable solver or real-market advice. Subgame perfection is classical Selten theory, not our invention; Osborne, Tamuz, MIT refinement lectures and Maryland's game-tree lecture ground the finite model, with Knight, Gambit and Nashpy credited as existing teaching and software. GameLab studies zero-sum matrices, Correlab one-shot advice and obedience, and AuctionLab allocation incentives; SubgameLab instead audits sequential contingent plans, full-player versus root-local deviations and credibility in every finite perfect-information subtree. Plan IDs are deterministic: lexicographic complete action assignments, not merged normal-form outcome classes, so plans that agree on outcomes are never folded together, and backward induction keeps unchosen child plans in the evidence instead of discarding them. The deployment got the same treatment as the reports: five live assets match merged source, the complete deployed Chrome workflow passes, the merged-main checks and the Pages deployment both passed, and the desktop hero, the phone chance-zero verdicts and the 320 px boundary witness tables were inspected by hand. Seeded trees vary in shape and repeated player ownership, some with chance roots, and the developed controls cover the six-decision and two-chance boundaries; the full source ledger and the API distinctions are in SOURCES.md.
  - Case study: [subgamelab.md](subgamelab.md)
  - Repo: [midya8/subgamelab](https://github.com/midya8/subgamelab)
